But they had forgotten where they had entered the sunk road that led nowhere in particular; followed it as it wound into exactly the middle of everywhere, and looking over its bank found the airy roofs of Starn lost behind a line of high black trees.

===Chinese===
他们在沙里跌跌撞撞往回走，循着来路。可他们忘了当初从哪儿拐进这条洼路的——它哪儿也不特别通向；只好跟着它七拐八绕，绕到恰恰是四方的正中央，从路坡上探头一望，斯塔恩那些轻盈的屋顶已经丢在一排高高的黑树后面了。

===Original===
Forgiveness woke in them and fatigue. Picus had been mocked for vanity, had mocked back, and set them off laughing. They were nervous, sweating, flushed. Felix shrieked when a snake flicked across his shoe. But it was all part of a game. Hardly able to see more than the sky, they trudged the road the sun had made into Danae’s chimney, down which God came in a shower of gold. Clarence led them. Picus followed. He had deprecated Carston’s suggestion that they should try and cut across the creek and make for the farm. “Bad bog,” he said. “Marsh-king’s sons,” said Felix, “that would be the end of us,” and Clarence that he had seen a pony lost there. At this choice of pleasures, Carston had followed them; and the sunk road, having come to the middle, stopped in a circular sandbank round a foul pond bordered with marsh-grass where there was no way out. They flung themselves down.

===Chinese===
他们心里醒来了宽恕，也醒来了疲乏。皮库斯先前因虚荣受人嘲弄，回敬了嘲弄，又把他们逗得大笑起来。他们神经紧张，汗流浃背，满脸通红。一条蛇嗖地掠过菲利克斯的鞋面，他尖叫了一声。可这一切都不过是游戏的一部分。天上之外几乎什么也看不见，他们吃力地走在那条太阳已烤成达那厄（Danae）烟囱的路上——当年神明就是化作一阵金雨，顺着它降下来的。克拉伦斯领头。皮库斯跟随。卡斯顿提议试着横穿水湾、直奔农庄，他不以为然。“烂泥沼，”他说。“沼泽王的儿子们，”菲利克斯说，“那咱们就全交待在那儿了。”克拉伦斯则说，他见过一匹小马陷死在那里。在这两般乐事之间，卡斯顿跟了他们；而那条洼路走到正中央，便到了头——一圈环形的沙堤，围着一只污浊的池塘，塘边镶满沼草，没有出路。他们一头栽倒在地。

===Original===
It occurred to Carston that it was all nonsense. They had only a few miles to go. Keeping towards the long hills, they must strike a road and eventually, Starn. At Starn there was water, tea, lime-juice, gin, champagne. The liverish grass and thick water displeased him. “We can’t stay forever in this cup,” he said.

===Chinese===
卡斯顿忽然觉得这一切荒唐透了。他们不过还有几英里路。朝着长长的山峦走下去，总能撞上一条大路，最终撞上斯塔恩。斯塔恩有水，有茶，有青柠汁，有杜松子酒，还有香槟。这肝色的草、稠浊的水，他看着就生厌。“咱们总不能永远泡在这只杯里，”他说。

===Original===
Picus was asleep, Clarence bending over him. An odd sort of pietà. Felix was sinking in the mud, collecting a plant he said was for Ross. All careful of each other’s needs and pleasures. He heard Clarence call softly: “Wet a handkerchief, Felix, I want it for his head.” Saw it brought over, and Picus jump up in a flash. “None of that sort of water for me, thanks.” To spite the men who took care of him? Probably. Carston felt that he was being given an opportunity to hate and that was good. “Hadn’t you better try and get us out of this?” The tall creature looked at him, sadly. “I’ll try; but you’ll have to leave it to me again, you know.” He sprang up the hollowed bank, kicking mouthfuls of sand into Carston’s face, and vanished. Carston crawled up to watch, hiding behind the heather rim. He saw Picus run a few yards and fling himself down on his face. He waited and saw him get up, heard him call “It’s this way,” saw him glance round and make off. Carston called back to the other three, heaved himself over, and they set off on a slight track towards the belt of wood.

===Chinese===
皮库斯睡着了，克拉伦斯俯在他身上。一幅古怪的圣殇图。菲利克斯正陷在泥里，采一株他说要带给罗斯（Ross）的草。人人都在照料彼此的需要和乐趣。他听见克拉伦斯轻声唤道：“把手绢打湿，菲利克斯，我要给他敷头。”看着手绢递过去，皮库斯一闪身跳了起来。“这种水给我免了，多谢。”存心气一气照顾他的人？多半是。卡斯顿觉得自己正被赐予一个恨的机会，而这很好。“你不如想法子带我们出去吧？”那高个子的生灵悲伤地望了他一眼。“我试试；可你们又得把事情全交给我了，这你们知道。”他一纵身蹿上塌陷的路坡，踢起一口口的沙子，扬了卡斯顿一脸，人便没了影。卡斯顿爬上去张望，躲在石楠镶边后面。他看见皮库斯跑出几码，一头扑倒在地。他等着，又见他爬起来，听见他喊“是这边”，看见他四下一瞥，拔腿去了。卡斯顿回头招呼另外三个，翻过坡去，一行人便循着一条浅浅的小径，朝那带树林进发。

===Original===
They went in. Pine-needles are not easy to walk on, like a floor of red glass. It is not cool under them, a black scented life, full of ants, who work furiously and make no sound. Something ached in Carston, a regret for the cool brilliance of the wood they had left, the other side of the hills, on the edge of the sea. This one was full of harp-noises from a wind when there was none outside. He saw Picus ahead, a shadow shifting between trunk and trunk. Some kind of woodcraft he supposed, and said so to Felix who said sleepily: “Somebody’s blunt-faced bees, dipping under the thyme-spray”; a sentence which made things start living again. Would they never have enough of what they called life? There was no kind of a track over the split vegetable glass. A place that made you wonder what sort of nothing went on there, year in year out.

===Chinese===
他们走了进去。松针不好走，像一层红玻璃铺就的地。针叶底下并不凉快，是一种黑色的、芬芳的生命，爬满蚂蚁，它们发疯似的忙碌，却不作一点声响。卡斯顿心里有什么东西在发疼，一阵懊悔，懊悔山那边、大海边上那片被他们丢下的清凉明澈的树林。这一片满是竖琴般的鸣响，来自一股外面根本没有的风。他看见皮库斯在前头，一道影子在树干与树干之间挪移。他想那总归是某种林中行路的本事，便这么对菲利克斯说了；菲利克斯睡意蒙眬地答：“谁家的钝脸蜜蜂，一头扎到百里香枝子底下去了。”这句话让万物重新活了起来。他们所谓的“生活”，他们就永远要不够么？在那碎开的草木玻璃上，没有半条路。这样一个地方，让你不由得琢磨：年复一年，这里进行着的究竟是哪一种“无”。

===Original===
The end of the wood was a little cliff, pitted with rabbit holes; and where the hills opened, Starn towering, not too far.

===Chinese===
树林的尽头是一面小崖，布满兔洞；山峦豁开的地方，斯塔恩高高耸峙，不算太远。

===Original===
“We get down here,” said Carston, trying the loose lip of the cliff.

===Chinese===
“咱们从这儿下去，”卡斯顿说着，探了探崖口那松动的唇沿。

===Original===
“Into what?” said Felix. Then they saw that the wood was surrounded again by marsh, the end of the creek which had separated them from Ross and Scylla, curled round on itself. Cutting them off from Starn this time. Clarence said: “Where’s Picus?” and Carston hoped that young man was about to get what was coming to him. Only he had gone. They began to think of their sins. Except Carston, who did not think of sins at all. There was raw heath on the other side, but the marsh was wide, and Carston was assured deadly. They left the little cliff’s edge, and reentered the wood. He heard Felix saying: “Where has Picus gone?” Gone to find Scylla? Carston wondered. By a quick turn which would bring him suddenly face to face. And at the folly of three strong young men, tramping about, lost and sapped.

===Chinese===
“下到哪儿去？”菲利克斯说。这时他们才看见，树林四周又是沼泽——正是先前把他们和罗斯、斯库拉（Scylla）隔开的那条水湾的尽头，此刻蜷了回来，兜住自身。这一回隔断的是斯塔恩。克拉伦斯说：“皮库斯呢？”卡斯顿巴望那小子马上领受他活该领受的东西。只是他已经走了。他们开始思量起自己的罪过。卡斯顿除外，他压根不思量罪过。对面是生荒原，可沼泽宽，而卡斯顿确信它致命。他们离开小崖的边沿，重新钻回树林。他听见菲利克斯说：“皮库斯上哪儿去了？”找斯库拉去了？卡斯顿寻思。抄个急转弯，猛地同她打照面。还有这份荒唐——三个强壮的年轻人，走来走去，迷了路，泄空了劲。

===Original===
“Call for him,” he said. “He’d not answer,” said Clarence﻿—“he may have had one of his bad pains again.” Carston wished one on him, making an image of him in his anger, until he thought he saw him, walking towards them, transparent and powerful, malicious and shy; his hat perched, to remind them of hooded birds. Remembered his magic, and forgave him. Grew angry again and dazed. Envious also, because he had no part in Picus’s dance. “Leave him alone,” he said, “he’s gone to find a way.” Clarence put a hand on his shoulder; thanks, he supposed, for taking it like that.

===Chinese===
“喊他一声吧，”他说。“他不会应的，”克拉伦斯说——“他没准儿又犯他那阵剧痛了。”卡斯顿巴不得他真犯上一犯，怒气里给他造出一幅像来，造到后来，竟像真看见了他：正朝他们走来，透明而有力，恶毒而羞怯；帽子翘在头顶上，提醒人想起那些戴兜帽的鸟。想起了他的魔法，便原谅了他。跟着又恼起来，又发昏。还嫉妒，因为皮库斯的那支舞里没有他的份。“别管他了，”他说，“他找路去了。”克拉伦斯把一只手搭上他的肩；他猜这是道谢，谢他把这口气咽得这么顺当。

===Original===
At last, from the extreme end of the wood, in the least propitious place, they saw across the marsh a clear causeway of stone.

===Chinese===
终于，从树林最尽头、最不像有路的地方，他们望见沼泽对面一道清晰的石头堤道。

===Original===
They scrambled down, crossed it, found a path which led them under the hills by a white road into Starn.

===Chinese===
他们攀下去，过了堤道，找到一条小径，引着他们贴着山根，沿一条白色大路走进斯塔恩。

===Original===
About the end of the walk, the less said the better, until at the inn the landlord answered their faint cries for drink. He wanted to know why they had left six and come back three.

===Chinese===
这最后一段路怎么走完的，还是少说为妙——直到客栈里，店主人应了他们讨水喝的微弱呼喊。他想知道，为什么他们六个出去，只回来了三个。

===Original===
“Lost ’em,” said Felix.

===Chinese===
“丢了，”菲利克斯说。

===Original===
“You should have stuck to Mr. Tracy. He’s the one for the heath. Knows all the paths, and the people who bide out there.”

===Chinese===
“你们该咬住特雷西先生（Mr. Tracy）不放的。论荒原，他是头一份的人物。条条小路都认得，连住在那上头的人也都认得。”

===Original===
The talk petered out. The evening marched gravely down. The two undoubtable shops put up shutters. The tourists had gone. A man came through with a bunch of sheep. The bar of the labourer’s inn turned on a gramophone. From time to time, a huge man came in from the fields, making for his beer. The cries of children stopped. Mothers came out. A fast touring-car shot across the square. A mist rose, an intangible gauze, refreshing them. They dined in the inn garden. When Carston saw in the west a large star watching, he though of the lights on Broadway. Felix saluted the star. Clarence worried, but without, for once, worrying them.

===Chinese===
谈话渐渐泄了气。暮色庄重地行军下来。那两家不容置疑的铺子上起了门板。游客们已经走了。一个汉子赶着一小群羊打广场穿过。佣工客栈的酒吧间拧开了留声机。不时有个五大三粗的汉子从地里进来，直奔他的啤酒。孩子们的哭喊停了。母亲们走了出来。一辆高速旅行车射过广场。雾气升起来，一层不可捉摸的轻纱，让他们为之一爽。他们在客栈花园里用了晚餐。卡斯顿望见西天一颗大星守望着，想起了百老汇（Broadway）的灯火。菲利克斯向那颗星行了个礼。克拉伦斯在发愁，只是这一回没有让他们跟着愁。

===Original===
They set out to watch the square again.

===Chinese===
他们又出来守望广场。

===Original===
Then Scylla appeared, and Picus and Ross.

===Chinese===
接着斯库拉出现了，还有皮库斯和罗斯。

===Original===
“We found him, sitting on a stone, saying he had lost you: that you ran away from him on Hangar’s ridge.”

===Chinese===
“我们找到了他，坐在一块石头上，说自己把你们丢了：说你们在哈恩加山脊（Hangar’s ridge）上甩下他跑了。”

===Original===
“He lost you first,” said Ross. “I don’t blame you.”

===Chinese===
“是他先把你们弄丢的，”罗斯说。“我不怪你们。”

===Original===
“He did,” said Felix, “and cleared off when he’d made a final mess of it. I know his ways. Have you got the mead?”

===Chinese===
“他就是，”菲利克斯说，“等把一切搅成个彻底的烂摊子，便一溜了之。他那套我清楚。蜂蜜酒你们拿到了吗？”

===Original===
“Bottles,” said Scylla.

===Chinese===
“成瓶的，”斯库拉说。

===Original===
They were surprised when Picus, who had thrown himself into a chair, got up and said: “That’s how you take it. It was not my fault.”

===Chinese===
皮库斯本来一头栽进一把椅子，这时竟站起来说：“原来你们是这么看这件事的。那不是我的错。”众人都吃了一惊。

===Original===
Clarence sprang beside him.

===Chinese===
克拉伦斯一跃到了他身边。

===Original===
As he went out, Scylla touched his sleeve with her fingers. He took no notice, but Clarence did.

===Chinese===
他往外走时，斯库拉用手指碰了碰他的衣袖。他没理会，克拉伦斯却看在了眼里。

===Original===
Carston’s fatigue had passed into charity. They were all overtired, of course. The developments of over-fatigue were caprice and anger. What had Picus been up to? Helping himself with accidents, of course. But exasperating himself.

===Chinese===
卡斯顿的疲惫已经化成了慈悲。他们全都过累了，这是自然的。过累往下发展，便是反复无常和火气。皮库斯一直在弄什么名堂？当然是借着一桩桩意外给自己取利。可也把他自己气了个半死。

===Original===
“There were no bones broken,” he said, “you’ve got the mead, and we’ve all got back.” He was furious when Clarence ignored him, and went out into the half-light after the man who seemed to combine all the elements of a family curse. Felix began:

===Chinese===
“又没伤筋断骨，”他说，“蜂蜜酒也到手了，咱们也全都回来了。”克拉伦斯只当他不存在，他火透了，便走进半明半暗里，去追那个仿佛把一份家族诅咒的所有成分集于一身的男人。菲利克斯开了腔：

===Original===
“God! I’m sick of this. Why can’t we be at Biarritz leading a reasonable life?”

===Chinese===
“天哪！我烦透了。咱们干嘛不能去比亚里茨（Biarritz），过一点讲道理的日子？”

===Original===
A negro song came into Carston’s head: Bear your burden in the heat of the day: but it did not occur to him to tell it to the boy, whom it would have helped.

===Chinese===
一支黑人歌谣浮上卡斯顿心头：在白昼的酷热里背负你的重担。可他没想到把它说给那男孩听，虽然这歌本可以帮到他。

===Original===
Clarence came in again. “I’ve ordered the car. We ought to get back. I’ll tell the others.” Carston saw him go into the dining-room and heard Scylla call:

===Chinese===
克拉伦斯又进来了。“我叫了车。我们该回去了。我去告诉其他人。”卡斯顿看见他走进餐室，听见斯库拉喊：

===Original===
“Well, we want to eat. Tell it to wait.”

===Chinese===
“呃，我们还要吃饭呢。叫它等着。”

===Original===
“We must go home.”

===Chinese===
“我们必须回家。”

===Original===
“Take it home then, and send it back for us.”

===Chinese===
“那就让它回家去，再打发它回来接我们好了。”

===Original===
Silence again. Question of expense.

===Chinese===
又静了下来。这是开销的问题。

===Original===
Carston saw Picus come into the dining-room from the garden and sit down at a table by himself. He heard Scylla call:

===Chinese===
卡斯顿看见皮库斯从花园走进餐室，独自在一张桌旁坐下。他听见斯库拉喊：

===Original===
“Clarence, leave your fancy-boy alone.”

===Chinese===
“克拉伦斯，别去烦你的小情郎了。”

===Original===
That had done it.

===Chinese===
这一句坏了事。

===Original===
Picus was eating alone. Anything might happen, only it was time for sleep. Half an hour later they had packed into the car and shot away, up into the hills the night wind had now made exquisite, to a different wood from the one in whose red-glass darkness Picus had lost them, moist and shimmering, a repetition of the tremblings of the stars.

===Chinese===
皮库斯独自吃着。什么事都可能发生，只是到了该睡的时辰。半小时后，他们已经塞进汽车，疾驰而去，冲进夜风此刻已吹拂得精美绝伦的群山，驶向另一片树林——不再是皮库斯弄丢他们的那一片红玻璃的幽暗——湿润，闪着微光，恰似繁星的一阵阵震颤又重演了一遍。

## Lydia / 莉迪亚

===Original===
She was alone next morning. Philip had gone out to meet a Jew whose favour they were nursing. She had refused, felt she no longer cared if he mismanaged it. She had not spoken since an hour after Scylla had left, and in that hour they had said worse things to each other than they had said to her. But Philip, who had almost cried with fear, in the morning was not dissatisfied. One does not leave the gutter without a few knocks. He had his own plans, his own adventure. Hoped from his heart Scylla was marrying the man. That would get them out of his way for good.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，她独自一人。菲利普（Philip）出门去了，去见一位他们正小心笼络的犹太人。她推掉了，觉得哪怕他把这桩事情办砸，她也全不在乎了。自斯库拉（Scylla）走后一小时起，她就再没开过口；就在那一小时里，他们彼此说出的难听话，比对她说过的还要难听。可是菲利普——那个几乎吓哭了的人——到了早晨倒并非不满意。人要爬出阴沟，总免不了挨几下磕碰。他自有他的打算，自有他的冒险。他打心眼里盼着斯库拉就要嫁给那个男人了。那样就能叫他们从此不再碍他的事。

===Original===
Lydia sat at her writing-table, without her mask, either of love or makeup. Her head, still disfigured, did not belong to this age. She wrote:

===Chinese===
莉迪亚（Lydia）坐在写字台前，卸下了面具——无论是爱的面具，还是脂粉的面具。她的头脸依旧带着毁损，全然不属于这个时代。她写道：

===Original===
> My dear Clarence,
> How are you all?
> Scylla is up and dined last night. She seemed very well and a little mysterious. I understood, though I may have got it quite wrong, that you’re thinking of marrying each other.
> Please let Phil and me know if it’s true. It almost hurts one’s feelings not to be the first to wish you luck.
> No chance of getting away until Phil has pulled off some more business, and then he wants to go to Eastbourne!

===Chinese===
> 亲爱的克拉伦斯（Clarence）：
> 你们大家都好吗？
> 斯库拉已经下床，昨晚还下来用了餐。她看上去很好，还带几分神秘。我了解到——尽管我也许完全会错了意——你们俩是在打算结婚。
> 若真有其事，请让我和菲尔（Phil）知道。不能头一个祝你们好运，简直叫人心里不是滋味。
> 菲尔没再做成几桩买卖，我们就没有脱身的指望；到那时，他还想去伊斯特本（Eastbourne）呢！

===Original===
She went out herself and posted it to Tollerdown.

===Chinese===
她亲自出门，把信寄往了托勒当（Tollerdown）。

===Original===
Once, down South, one of the boys had called Scylla “bird-alone.” They had all asked for names. Picus had been cat-by-himself. Felix, *l’œuf sur le toit*. Ross, bird-catcher. They had quarrelled a little that morning, and she had not been pleased when Ross had said, grinning: “If Scylla’s the bird, one might call you ‘wolf-alone.’ ”

===Chinese===
有一回在南方，男孩们中的一个管斯库拉叫"孤鸟"。于是人人都讨了自己的诨名。皮库斯（Picus）是"独行猫"。菲利克斯（Felix）是"屋顶上的蛋"（*l’œuf sur le toit*）。罗斯（Ross）是"捕鸟人"。那天早晨他们小小拌了一场嘴；罗斯咧嘴说了句"要是斯库拉是那只鸟，倒尽可以叫你‘孤狼’"，她听了很不受用。

## Mr. Tracy / 特雷西先生

===Original===
Carston spent the next morning thinking about old Mr. Tracy, or, more exactly, how he would hate to walk up his drive. In his country he would have faced a dozen of them, but he had been out early to scout and had seen the house up a much-too-long-yellow avenue between high clipped shrubs. Unsympathetic. Like a long neck into a trap.

===Chinese===
第二天上午，卡斯顿（Carston）一直盘算着老特雷西先生——或者更确切地说，盘算着自己会多么讨厌走上他家的车道。换在他自己的国家，这样的宅门他能一口气闯上一打；可他一早出门踩过点，看见那宅子立在一条长得过分的黄沙林荫道尽头，夹道是修剪得整整齐齐的高篱。不投缘。活像一条长脖子，一直伸进陷阱里去。

===Original===
At half-past twelve he had an idea. At two o’clock precisely he had passed the lodge. At two minutes past he saw old Mr. Tracy leave his front door and halt, turning as he walked, to speak to someone within. Two seconds later he saw a neat painted gate in the laurel wall, the entrance of a tunnel. One second later he was mastering the latch, and had disappeared from sight.

===Chinese===
十二点半，他有了主意。两点整，他走过了门房。两点过两分，他看见老特雷西先生走出正门，走几步又回过身，同屋里的人说话。两秒钟后，他望见月桂树墙上一扇漆色鲜洁的小门，是一条穿篱甬道的入口。又过一秒，他已扣住门闩，消失得无影无踪。

===Original===
We will follow him, as earlier writers say so prettily, as he commences trespasser, in hiding from the approaching master of the house.

===Chinese===
让我们跟随着他——早年的作家们把这话说得多么动听——此刻他已然沦为擅入私地之徒，正躲着那位迎面而来的宅邸主人。

===Original===
He followed the tunnel about ten yards, where it led him on to a wood-path parallel with the drive, whose principal feature was a pavement of enormous roots. He listened to the crisp sound of Mr. Tracy’s boots, waited till he had passed, tripped over the roots, slipped down the tunnel again, and reached the house as though nothing had happened. He was ashamed until he noticed that it was a sound instinct that had made him avoid the old man in open.

===Chinese===
他顺着甬道走了十来码，甬道把他引上一条与车道平行的林间小径，那小径最显著的特色，是以巨大的树根铺成的路面。他听着特雷西先生靴子踩出的脆响，等他走远，便在树根上磕磕绊绊，重新溜进甬道，回到宅前，仿佛什么也不曾发生。他先还臊得慌，直到省悟过来：叫他避开与老人打照面的，原是一番健全的本能。

===Original===
Half an hour later he was in the library. He heard: “So you want to buy that mischief-making cup for an antiquarian friend in the States? And you want its pedigree? I can write you out the particulars, of course. My name counts for something, but I imagine that I was sufficiently precise last time we met.”

===Chinese===
半小时后，他已经待在书房里。他听见："这么说，你要替美国古董界的一位朋友买下那只惹是生非的杯子？还想要它的出身谱系？具体情形我当然可以写给你。我的名字也还作数，不过我想，上次见面时我已经说得够准确了。"

===Original===
“No, sir,” said Carston, “you were not. I have its photograph in the book that wasn’t burned as thoroughly as you might have wished. There it’s described as a mass-vessel, early English, from your collection. Now, I don’t give a damn which it is, but it can’t be that and a poison-cup. And before I write out a cheque I want to know which.”

===Chinese===
"不，先生，"卡斯顿说，"你并不准确。它的照片就在那本书里——那本书烧得不像您指望的那么干净。书里把它描述成一件早期英格兰的弥撒祭器，出自您的收藏。好了，它是哪一路东西我根本不在乎，可它不能既是那件祭器，又是一只毒杯。我开支票之前，想知道它到底是哪一个。"

===Original===
“But, in any case, Mr. Carston, while we are speaking of money, I imagine that any cheque should be made out to me.”

===Chinese===
"不过，无论如何，卡斯顿先生，既然谈到了钱，我想，任何支票都该抬头开给我。"

===Original===
“No, sir. I and three other persons heard you confirm your son’s gift of it to Miss Taverner.” (His reputation as a collector’s at stake. It can’t be two things at once)﻿—“As to a second opinion, I expect your British Museum could give us that.”

===Chinese===
"不，先生。我同另外三个人，亲耳听见您确认您儿子已把它赠予塔弗纳（Taverner）小姐。"（他收藏家的名声押在这上头。一件东西总不能同时是两样东西）——"至于第二份鉴定意见，我想贵国的大英博物馆就能给出。"

===Original===
“I admit,” said Mr. Tracy, “that it is probably a mass-cup. In my horror of loose feeling I preferred to suggest any origin, however grim or far-fetched, than that my relatives should abandon themselves to superstition.”

===Chinese===
"我承认，"特雷西先生说，"它多半是一只弥撒杯。我一向怕的就是滥情的松垮做派，宁可为它另派一个来历——多阴森、多牵强都在所不惜——也不愿让我的亲眷们放纵于迷信。"

===Original===
This might have sounded noble, but Carston kept on. Bit of bunk: what has he been up to? That’s what I’m not to find out. He said:

===Chinese===
这话未尝不显得高尚，可卡斯顿不依不饶。一派鬼话：他背地里到底干了些什么？这正是人家不肯让我查明白的。他说：

===Original===
“It seems to me that you’ve exchanged a fine, mysterious, almost sacred fable for a sordid, even brutal, personal invention. Facts are what I’m here for.”

===Chinese===
"依我看，您是拿一个卑琐、甚至残忍的私人杜撰，换下了一个精妙的、神秘的、近乎神圣的传说。我为事实而来。"

===Original===
What I won’t get, not the ones that matter.

===Chinese===
而我偏得不到，要紧的那些一件也到不了手。

===Original===
“You can be satisfied that in all probability the photograph describes it correctly. An early Church vessel, its shape suggests a chalice, with the setting lost. If so, it might well have been part of a crusader’s loot. Incidentally, since its probable origin greatly enhances its value, you might do well to stick to my earlier suggestion, the last part of which is no more than simple fact﻿—”

===Chinese===
"您大可放心，照片多半描述得不错。一件早期教会器皿，形制指向一只圣餐杯，托座已经失散。倘若如此，它很可能是十字军战利品的一部分。顺带说一句，既然这可能的来历大大抬升了它的身价，您不妨还是坚持我早先的说法——那说法的最后半句，不过是简单的事实——"

===Original===
“Cool!” Carston gasped. Cucumbers and icebergs.

===Chinese===
"真够冷的！"卡斯顿倒抽一口气。黄瓜似的凉，冰山似的冷。

===Original===
“﻿—Or is to be part of the price for Scylla Taverner’s hypothetical virginity?” Carston thought:

===Chinese===
"——再不然，它是不是要算作斯库拉·塔弗纳那份假想童贞的价钱的一部分？"卡斯顿心想：

===Original===
If I start losing my temper, it is he who will find things out. He chose blind-man’s bluff. We must play until we needn’t. At the same time conviction came to him that they would find out nothing. His direct attack was obvious, useless, unfruitful.

===Chinese===
我要是发起脾气来，把底细查个明白的就要成了他。他挑了蒙眼捉人的把戏。不到不必再玩的时候，就得一直玩下去。与此同时，他心里升起一个确信：他们什么也查不出来。他这正面强攻，痕迹太露，无用，无果。

===Original===
“I have nothing to say, but that I buy nothing with my eyes shut. And what I’ve come to get is your reasons for supposing it a Church vessel﻿—” Vain repetition. Not even taken the wind out of the old man’s sails. Sailing serenely on: through a weak position: through fraud.

===Chinese===
"我没什么可说的，只有一句：我不做闭着眼睛的买卖。我来要的，是您凭什么认定它是一件教会器皿——"白白重复了一遍。连老头儿帆里的风都夺不走一丝。人家稳稳当当照样扬帆：穿过一处破绽百出的阵脚：穿过一场欺诈。

===Original===
“You shall have them, Mr. Carston, tonight. Signed on my authority. You are staying at the Star?”

===Chinese===
"您今夜就能拿到，卡斯顿先生。以我的名义签署作保。您下榻在星宿旅馆（the Star）？"

## Picus / 皮库斯

===Original===
On the same night Picus went out, also alone and in a clearing among trees. English yews, black and untidy with their shoots that are never young, a yard of rusty twig, old as a house-high lime or beech.

===Chinese===
同一个夜晚，皮库斯（Picus）也出了门，同样独自一人，来到林中一片空地。英国的紫杉，黑黢黢、乱蓬蓬，抽出的枝条没有一点嫩相，尽是一码码锈色的细枝，苍老得不输房高的椴树和山毛榉。

===Original===
The space was diversified with the stone boxes of a country churchyard. Dressed for Piccadilly, Picus was lying face-down on his mother’s grave, on turf not dew-sown, but rain-sodden, overlooked by a sugar-white marble angel carrying an urn. He was telling the stalks and the worms and a snail how he loved her, and resented her death, and was troubled by its mystery.

===Chinese===
空地上错落着乡村教堂墓园的一具具石棺匣。皮库斯一身皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）的打扮，脸朝下伏在母亲的坟上；坟头的草皮不是洒了露水，而是叫雨水泡了个透，一尊糖霜般雪白的大理石天使捧着骨灰瓮，在坟上方俯看着他。他对草茎、对蠕虫、对一只蜗牛倾诉：他多么爱她，多么怨她的死，又为死亡的这个谜多么心烦意乱。

===Original===
“*Pretty mother, why did you do it?*”

===Chinese===
"*漂亮的妈妈，你为什么那样做？*"

===Original===
“*Why did that tart matter?*”

===Chinese===
"*那个浪蹄子有什么要紧的？*"

===Original===
“*What did you want me to do?*”

===Chinese===
"*你要我怎么做呢？*"

===Original===
“*Why do I hate all women?*”

===Chinese===
"*我为什么恨所有的女人？*"

===Original===
Once Nanna had made him ill when he had heard her say: “What did Mrs. Tracy mean by drowning herself with Mr. Picus a boy, and his father taking his child to the inquest and saying it was suicide?”

===Chinese===
有一回，娜娜（Nanna）一句话害他病了一场，他听见她说："特雷西太太到底安的什么心？小皮库斯先生还那么点儿大，她就投了水；他父亲又领着亲儿子去验尸，一口咬定那是自杀。"

===Original===
And Scylla’s answer: “People went in for sensibilities then.” Blaming his mother. Now he’d see if she had any. He grasped the turf mound and tried to shake it. Get mother out and make her tell him what had really happened and that he must never make anyone suffer as she had suffered. That was like his father and he must never be like his father﻿—The marble parody of a nymph went on offering him an urn. He tried to see if the lid would come off, but it was all in one meanly designed, badly chiselled piece. His father with his impeccable taste had stuck it up on purpose. Fattened on his son’s hatred. Had used the cup to make him wicked. To lose him the people who comforted him. Would go on living forever. The wires of the rust tin flower-box caught his cuff. Rain water in it, and no flowers. Can’t go daisy-hunting at midnight in the rain. That’s all mother had, an empty tin box with the rain in it and no flowers. He did not count his six feet of young man flung there.

===Chinese===
还有斯库拉（Scylla）的回答："那个年头的人，讲究的就是这份多愁善感。"这是怪他母亲。好啊，如今他倒要看看她自己有没有那份心肠。他攥住草皮坟包，使劲想把它摇开。把母亲摇出来，逼她告诉他真正出了什么事，告诉他：他万万不能叫任何人像她那样受苦。那是他父亲的做派，而他万万不能像他父亲——大理石雕的山林仙女，一件拙劣的仿品，还在朝他捧着那只瓮。他试着看瓮盖能不能掀开，可那是一件设计寒碜、凿工粗糙的整体货。他那品味无懈可击的父亲，是存心把它竖在这儿的。靠儿子的恨把自己养肥了。用那只杯子把他教坏。害他失去那些安慰他的人。还要一直活下去，活到永远。生锈铁皮花箱的铁丝挂住了他的袖口。箱里积着雨水，没有花。总不能半夜里冒雨去采雏菊。母亲就只有这个：一只空铁箱，盛着雨，没有花。他没有把自己那六英尺的年轻身子算在内——它就那么抛掷在坟上。

===Original===
“You’d no business to go off and leave me like that. Gives me nothing to do but want you back. Just for a minute to tell me something: put kick into me again. Mummy, don’t you see: you gave the old boy the game? You were so much prettier: might have stayed and seen me through. Now you’ve made me be a bitch.”

===Chinese===
"你真不该就那么一走了之，把我一个人丢下。害得我无事可做，只想把你讨回来。哪怕就回来一分钟，跟我说句什么：再往我心里添一把劲儿。妈妈，你难道不明白：你把那一局白白送给了老头儿？你比他标致多了：本可以留下来，看着我熬出头。这下好了，你把我变成了一个混蛋。"

===Original===
He sat there weeping and considering how he had been a bitch and couldn’t stop. Didn’t know how to find old Clarence and Scylla again and love them and see that they loved him. Clarence now. One of his suspicions was that the change in Clarence was his fault. Why wasn’t it entirely the man’s own fault if he chose to go gaga and mope about like a frustrated hen? Funny. They used to call Clarence a bit too handsome, like a super-chorus-boy. Now you might say like a butler with a past. Tell him that. Now Felix beginning to act like a toad. Good old Carston. And great Ross. Hats off to Ross, doing nothing at all but what he had to. He remembered their old conviction that Ross had some sort of stable tip in invisible affairs. “*Won’t tell. Says we can look at his pictures.*”

===Chinese===
他坐在那里流着泪，寻思自己怎么就当了个混蛋，还收不住。不知道怎样才能重新找回老克拉伦斯（Clarence）和斯库拉，去爱他们，也让他们爱他。就说克拉伦斯吧。他的疑心之一，就是克拉伦斯身上的变化是他造成的。可那人要是自己乐意变得疯疯傻傻，乐意像只孵蛋落空的母鸡那样终日蔫头耷脑，凭什么不干脆全怪他自己？可笑。从前大家总说克拉伦斯漂亮得过了头，像大型歌舞队里的头号美少年。如今你不妨说，像个有一段不清白往事的管家。这话你倒说给他听。如今连菲利克斯（Felix）也开始摆起癞蛤蟆的做派了。好样的老卡斯顿。还有伟大的罗斯（Ross）。向罗斯脱帽——人家除了非做不可的事，什么都不做。他想起他们从前的定见：罗斯在无形之事上，总握着几分马房内线般的可靠消息。"*他不肯说。只说我们可以看他的画。*"

===Original===
“*We should have been all right together, Scylla and I, and now the sight of her scares me stiff. Lovely and witty, and decent and passionate and kind. There’s a figure that stands sentinel before her. I can’t see its face. Not a chap you’d care to meet. If I looked at him, he’d go.*”

===Chinese===
"*本来，斯库拉和我，我们俩在一起会好好的；可如今一看见她，我就吓得僵在那里。可爱，机智，正派，热烈，又善良。她面前立着一个守哨的身影。我看不见它的脸。不是那种你乐意见着的人。我要是朝它看过去，它就会走开。*"

===Original===
“*These things matter a devil of a lot, and they don’t matter at all.*”

===Chinese===
"*这些事要紧得要命，又全然不要紧。*"

===Original===
“*Scylla’s a darling. Want my Scylla.*”

===Chinese===
"*斯库拉是个可人儿。我要我的斯库拉。*"

===Original===
“*Mother, why can’t I have Scylla?*”

===Chinese===
"*妈妈，为什么我不能得到斯库拉？*"

===Original===
The angel went on holding the urn. He kicked the turf till a sod tore off. A glowworm turned its back on him. By infinite degrees the green gem moved off.

===Chinese===
天使照旧捧着那只瓮。他一脚脚踢着草皮，直到踢落一块草墩。一只萤火虫拿背对着他。那颗绿宝石以无穷缓慢的刻度，一点点挪远了。

===Original===
“*And for half a minute I thought that cup had come to light us up a bit.*” He whistled “Waiting for the Moon to Rise.”

===Chinese===
"*而且有那么半分钟，我还以为那只杯子是来给我们添点亮光的。*"他吹起口哨，吹的是《等月升起》（Waiting for the Moon to Rise）。

===Original===
“*And it was the old man after all!*”

===Chinese===
"*闹了半天，原来还是那老头儿！*"

===Original===
“*And Carston shan’t have Scylla if he does call the old man’s bluff.*”

===Chinese===
"*就算卡斯顿当真戳穿了老头儿的把戏，他也休想把斯库拉弄到手。*"

===Original===
He had the usual difficulty in extracting the glowworm from the grass.

===Chinese===
他照例费了一番周折，才把那只萤火虫从草丛里请了出来。

===Original===
“*Go and light up mummy’s tomb.*” He stuck it round the knob of the urn, where it fell off. He kicked the muffled earth that squelched, the tears pouring down, till he found a cache of pebbles under his heel and pelted the marble female in the thick dark, and then he had another idea, and with a handful in his pocket went across, round and over the graves, to a low, unlighted house, and tossed them up against a window. A head poked out:

===Chinese===
"*去把妈妈的坟墓照亮吧。*"他把它按到瓮顶的圆球上，它却掉了下来。他踢着那吃透了雨、噗叽作响的闷土，眼泪哗哗直淌，直到脚跟底下踩出一批藏着的石子，他便在浓黑里拿它们掷那大理石的女人；接着又生出一个新主意，往口袋里揣上一把，绕过、跨过一座座坟茔，走向一栋低矮无灯的房子，把石子一颗颗抛上了窗。一颗脑袋探了出来：

===Original===
“What’s that?”

===Chinese===
"谁呀？"

===Original===
“Me.”

===Chinese===
"是我。"

===Original===
“Come in, my dear boy, come in.” An old man in a nightshirt opened his front-door and let him in.

===Chinese===
"进来吧，亲爱的孩子，进来吧。"一个穿长睡衣的老人打开前门，让他进去了。

## Ross / 罗斯

===Original===
Ross finished the drawing of a plant that grew in whorls and spirals and tendrils and bracts. The naturalist had trained the painter. He copied it exactly. Tomorrow he would do it differently, double it, halve it, add six to it, make a picture out of repetitions of a stalk.

===Chinese===
罗斯画完了一幅植物的图样，那植物的枝叶以轮生、螺旋、卷须和苞片的方式生长。训练出这位画家的，是那位博物学家。他分毫不差地照实描摹。到了明天，他会换个画法：把它翻倍，把它减半，给它添上六株，用一根花茎的种种重复排成一幅画。

===Original===
It was very late. The hardly perceptible noise of his pencil was all he had for company. He went into the kitchen to look for bread and cheese. A candle was lit in a tin lantern, there was a fire-stain on the floor. Nanna, long in bed, had left her sewing.

===Chinese===
夜已经很深了。铅笔那几乎察觉不出的沙沙声，是他唯一的伴。他走进厨房去找面包和干酪。马口铁提灯里点着一支蜡烛，地板上留着一处生火的渍痕。娜娜早已上了床，把针线活撂在了那里。

===Original===
> There was an old woman and she sat spinning,
 

 And still she sat and still she span, and still she wished for company.

===Chinese===
> 从前有个老婆婆，坐在那里纺纱，
 

 她还是坐着、还是纺呀纺，还是盼着有人作伴。

===Original===
He grinned at the old nurse’s horror-story, went back to the studio and watered one of the shallow pans stuck with the seeds he had gathered tramping Europe. A bee-orchid had come up: an odd-scented herb from a pass on the Pyrenees: a rare lily was over. Whenever he touched it life grew. Plants and dogs and children. Eggs hatched. And men? They were there to make him laugh. If they found rest in him, he was indifferent as Nature, and in general as kind.

===Chinese===
他对老保姆那个骇人的故事咧嘴一笑，回到画室，给一只浅浅的育苗盘浇了水——盘里插着他徒步漫游欧洲时采来的种子。一株蜜蜂红门兰（bee-orchid）冒了出来；一株气味古怪的香草，来自比利牛斯（Pyrenees）山上的一处山口；一株珍稀的百合则已经开过。每当他碰一碰，生命便滋长起来。植物、狗和孩子。蛋也孵了出来。那么人呢？人活在世上是为了逗他发笑。倘若有人在他身上寻得安息，他便如大自然一般淡漠，大体上也一样仁慈。

===Original===
He loosened the earth pricked by a hairy spike, tossed a pebble away, opened a window. A bird disturbed brushed him without alarm. An owl sailed by, curved over at the house-corner and skimmed down the wood. A dark grey night, brimming with business. His portfolio under his arm for a picture-book, he went up to bed.

===Chinese===
他松了松被一根毛茸茸的尖刺戳过的泥土，拈走一颗小石子，推开一扇窗。一只受惊的鸟擦着他掠过，并不慌张。一只猫头鹰悠然滑过，在屋角上空弯转身子，贴着树林掠了下去。一个深灰色的夜，满满当当都是营生。他腋下夹着画夹——那是为一本画册预备的——上楼睡觉去了。

## Scylla / 斯库拉

===Original===
She arrived in London at the time when all reasonable citizens are trying to leave it, and the place seems fuller than ever. Full of townspeople shown up by the magnificence of summer, with children who appear brutalised from want of contact with things growing; where, in spite of every grit-weathered leaf, there is a pretence made that all is for the best inside the vast, roaring, fortuitous wilderness: that Epping Forest is the true green wood, and Southend virgin sea. If Paris is a lovely salon displayed for conversation, London is a lumber-room to be foraged for junk, rubbish, white elephants, treasure. Midsummer is not the time to do it.

===Chinese===
她抵达伦敦的时候，正当所有通情达理的市民都在设法逃离它，而这地方看上去比以往任何时候都更满。满城都是被夏日的盛大照出原形的城里人，身边带着些因亲近不到生长之物而显得被作践坏了的孩子；在这里，尽管片片叶子都蒙着风沙，人们仍然做出一副姿态，仿佛在这片浩大、轰响、全凭偶然的荒野里一切都再好不过：埃平森林（Epping Forest）便是真正的绿林，绍森德（Southend）便是未凿的处女海。如果说巴黎是一间为清谈而陈设的可爱沙龙，伦敦便是一间听凭翻拣的杂物间，供人刨寻破烂、垃圾、弃之可惜的累赘和财宝。仲夏可不是干这营生的时节。

===Original===
Scylla walked through the green park, fresh from no substitute hills and the sea, and not in the least grateful for them. Yet with only contempt for the posters and pretence that represented the Londoners’ poor escape to the land. Her yellow lawn frock blew up over her knees, under her powdered arms and throat there was a faint gold patina of freckles. Her little neck-scarf flying out behind her, she walked like a nymph in a temper, blessing nothing she passed.

===Chinese===
斯库拉（Scylla）穿过那座绿意葱茏的公园，刚从那绝非替身的山峦与大海那边回来，对它们没有半分感恩。然而，对那些招贴和虚饰——它们代表着伦敦人逃向乡土的那点寒酸出逃——她只剩轻蔑。她那件细麻纱的黄裙子被风吹得翻过膝盖，扑了粉的手臂和喉咙底下，浮着一层淡金色的雀斑薄晕。小小的颈巾在身后飘飞，她走起来像个正在使性子的宁芙，一路所过，不祝福任何东西。

===Original===
Something had been taken away from her. Not Picus or Felix, but what they had made her think about. Apples of Iduna the goddess, given her to feed the Aesir, without which she pined without dying. What happened to Iduna and her apples after? Loki, Saturday, had stolen them and shut her up in a giant’s castle, and she had been waited on by elf-women, very pleasant in front, but round the back made out of hollow boards. Beastly hot day and no adventures. Business at grey offices in Lincoln’s Inn. The only woman she really liked married to a boy she did not. She was going to dinner there.

===Chinese===
有什么东西被人从她身上夺走了。不是皮库斯（Picus），也不是菲利克斯（Felix），而是他们曾令她思索的那些东西。女神伊登（Iduna）的苹果，交给她去奉养阿萨神族（Aesir）；失了苹果，她便憔悴而不死。后来伊登和她的苹果又怎样了呢？洛基（Loki）——星期六——把它们偷了去，把她关进一座巨人的城堡，叫一群精灵女子伺候她，个个正面看去十分可人，转到背后却全是空心木板拼成的。热得要命的鬼天气，一桩奇遇也无。在林肯律师学院（Lincoln's Inn）的灰色办公室里办公事。她真心喜欢的唯一一个女人，嫁给了她不喜欢的一个男孩。她正要上那儿去吃晚饭。

===Original===
She remembered that the woman, her friend Lydia, had wanted once to marry Clarence. Might have married him until, one day, Clarence had made a scene: said that he could not leave Picus: that Picus needed him: had told Lydia that she did not love him: that it was a trick to get a husband: and had broken down badly after. Lydia had said nothing. And “had never been quite the same since.” That said it exactly. Had probably married her slick young outsider to annoy Clarence. A real chorus-boy beauty with a spirit to match. She would dine there, in their pretentious flat, all shams. It pleased Lydia if her friends flattered her husband. Scylla knew that it might please her that evening if she showed contempt.

===Chinese===
她记得，这个女人——她的朋友莉迪亚（Lydia）——曾经一度想嫁给克拉伦斯（Clarence）。本来也许真嫁了他，直到有一天，克拉伦斯大闹了一场：说他离不开皮库斯：说皮库斯需要他：还告诉莉迪亚说她并不爱他：说那不过是钓一个丈夫的把戏：事后又垮得一塌糊涂。莉迪亚什么也没说。而且“从那以后就再没完全复原过”。这话说得再准不过。她多半是嫁了她那个油滑的外路年轻人，好气一气克拉伦斯。一副地道的歌舞队小生相貌，配着一副正好相称的心性。她就要上那儿吃晚饭去了，在他们那套讲排场的公寓里，件件是假货。朋友们奉承她丈夫，莉迪亚便高兴。斯库拉知道，那天晚上她若摆出轻蔑，倒也许正中她的下怀。

===Original===
So no sweet temper adorned her as she swung into the new sitting-room with its faked cabinets and painful majolicas, and saw Lydia in a too-short frock and a too-tight hair wave, and a too-pink makeup, reading the *Romaunt de la Rose*. A woman bred out of great stone castles for a life of power and danger, she looked a fool, stripped of what should have been on her, the formal setting that should have extended north, south, east, and west of her. Not necessarily castles. A bare table and a window stuffed with sacking might have suited her purpose, when the purpose was her own, not a stunt to please her husband, like a lion riding a bicycle at a fair.

===Chinese===
于是，当她一阵风似的走进那间新客厅——里头摆着做假的橱柜和刺眼的马约利卡彩陶——看见莉迪亚穿着一条过短的裙子、烫着一头过紧的卷发、抹着一层过红的脂粉，正在读《玫瑰传奇》（*Romaunt de la Rose*）时，她身上便没有什么好脾性可作装点。一个本该从巍峨石堡中养出来、过惯权势与险境生涯的女人，此刻看着像个傻子，被剥去了本该披挂在她身上的东西——那套本该向她东西南北四面延展开去的正式镶嵌。倒未必非是城堡不可。一张光板桌、一扇塞满麻袋布的窗，也许反倒称她的意，只要那用意是她自己的，而不是讨丈夫欢心的噱头——像集市上骑自行车的狮子。

===Original===
Vexed deliberation marked the ivory forehead, her chief beauty. Her stockings were not drawn tight, and did not match. An intelligence made for children and learning and administration was adapting itself to marriage, with a gigolo, in a shaky business, in London, without capital, after the war. Would do it badly unless she broke him. Could not break him, he would twist and slip off.

===Chinese===
恼人的筹谋刻在那象牙色的额头上——她最美的地方。她的长袜没有绷紧，两只还不是一对儿。一副为儿女、为学问、为经营管理而造的智识，正在适应一桩婚姻：同一个吃软饭的小白脸，守着一桩摇摇欲坠的生意，在伦敦，没有本钱，又是战后。这桩事她会办得很糟，除非她把他降服。可她降服不了他，他会一扭身滑脱。

===Original===
A cathedral had better not turn mousetrap, or a chalice a cocktail shaker. A ten-inch gun should not be trained on a mark that is not there.

===Chinese===
一座大教堂还是别改成捕鼠夹的好，一只圣餐杯也别拿去当鸡尾酒摇壶。一尊十英寸炮不该去瞄准一个并不在那里的靶子。

===Original===
And Scylla found that all she could do was laugh to see her friend so much in love.

===Chinese===
而斯库拉发现，眼看朋友爱得这般深，她能做的唯有发笑。

===Original===
And Lydia knew this. Also that Scylla had kept her freedom, was up to all their old amusements out in Europe, down in the South. Scylla saw Clarence continually and made fun of him. Might flirt with him, more curious things can happen, when her proper business was to marry too, and establish herself.

===Chinese===
莉迪亚也知道这个。还知道斯库拉保住了自己的自由，他们当年在欧洲各地、在南方的那些老消遣，她样样玩得转。斯库拉不断同克拉伦斯见面，拿他打趣。说不定还同他调情——更蹊跷的事也是会有的——虽说她的正经营生，本也该是嫁人，是安身立命。

===Original===
And both the young women knew that this meeting if inevitable was unfortunate, the end of a friendship from university to marriage. Lydia had made a dangerous one. God only knew where adventure would lead the other.

===Chinese===
两个年轻女人都明白，这次会面纵然免不了，却是不幸的，是一场从大学绵延到嫁人的友谊的终局。莉迪亚择下的是一步险棋；天知道冒险会把另一个引到哪里去。

===Original===
The husband came in. Knocked over his wife’s book with a movie paper, and began to talk about himself. A row at the garage and how he had scored. Lydia frowned.

===Chinese===
丈夫进来了。用一份电影画报打翻了太太的书，便开始谈他自己。车行里吵了一架，他如何大获全胜。

===Original===
“Phil, Scylla is here.” He kissed Scylla several times, while she stared up to see what the prettier woman could do with her eyes; while she was loathing him because he had taken her friend away from her. To use Lydia’s practical brain and her unpractised love for his own little ends, to betray her, mishandle her, exploit her. And be dreadfully punished when Lydia recovered from her passion because he had laid familiar paws on her pride. Her heaven-born pride which might as easily move to hell. In a timeless instant she saw the woman Lydia would be, when she would punish her fancy-boy for being the slick little animal he was. And, during the transition, break both hearts.

===Chinese===
“菲尔（Phil），斯库拉来了。”他吻了斯库拉好几下，她则抬眼去看，那位更漂亮的女人会拿她的眼睛做些什么；同时心里憎恶着他，因为他把她的朋友从她身边夺走了。拿莉迪亚那颗务实的头脑和她未经世故的爱情，去填他自己那点小小的欲壑；背叛她，作践她，盘剥她。等莉迪亚从热恋里醒过来，他会因为把放熟的爪子搭上了她的骄傲，而遭到可怕的报应。她那份天赋的、天堂似的骄傲，也同样轻易就转向地狱。在一个没有时间的瞬间里，她看见了莉迪亚将要成为的那个女人——到那时，她会惩罚她的小情郎，罚他天生就是那副油滑的小畜生。而在转变的当口，碎掉的是两颗心。

===Original===
*I may become like that, too.* A thought passing, passionate then dispassionate.

===Chinese===
*我也可能变成那样。*一个念头掠过，先是激切，随即平静。

===Original===
At lunch, on Philip’s insistence, she praised the table-setting, who had adored Lydia for being the world’s worst housekeeper. It was easier when Philip dropped the garage and making eyes, and showed frank jealousy. He was really afraid of his wife’s old friends, knew that he must detach her from them quickly. And Lydia revelled in his authority, her mind storing it up for later, for part of the interminable, intolerable score they would have to recite to one another.﻿ ﻿… In a house where there would be no children, nor any garden for forgiveness full of the other’s favourite flowers.

===Chinese===
午餐时，菲利普（Philip）非要她夸，她只好夸了那桌杯盘的陈设——她本来偏偏钟爱莉迪亚那全天下最不济的持家本事。等到菲利普撇下车行的话题、不再挤眉弄眼，露出一副不加掩饰的醋意时，日子反倒好熬些。他实在是怕太太的那些旧友，知道必须赶紧把她从他们身边扯开。莉迪亚则对他的专断受用不已，心里一笔笔记下，留待日后——留作他们俩往后不得不彼此背诵的那部没完没了、忍无可忍的怨谱的一部分。……在一座房子里，那里面将不会有孩子，也不会有一座用来宽恕的花园，种满对方心爱的花。

===Original===
Now Scylla minded this. Minded also that Philip had not even thought to approach her as his wife’s friend. What was left her now but observation? She had had enough of grief. There was only her amusement left, the contrast between Lydia’s naive eroticism and her formidable wits: Philip’s technique with her no more than the length of rope on which he had to hang himself. His method was to cut conversation, to interrupt whatever was said; and when he spoke, interrupt himself, so that there should never be any continuity. Perfectly sound. The quickest way to exasperate Scylla. He was reckoning that he could, not quite such a fool as these grand ladies thought him. Could show them that not being a gentleman was worth something: give Lydia’s lady friend something else to call him than a misplaced insect.

===Chinese===
这一下斯库拉把这事放在了心上。放在心上的还有：菲利普竟然压根没想到以“我太太的朋友”的身份来同她亲近。如今除了冷眼旁观，还给她剩下什么？悲伤她已经受够了。剩下的只有她的乐趣：莉迪亚天真直露的情欲与她那副厉害头脑之间的反差；还有菲利普降伏她的手段——不过是他日后自缢要用的那截绳长。他的办法是砍断交谈，别人说什么都要打岔；他自己开口时，也要打断自己，好教任何话头都续不上茬。十分在理。也是最快激恼斯库拉的办法。他打的主意是他应付得下来——他可不完全是这些阔太太眼里的那种傻瓜。要让她们瞧瞧，不是绅士也自有价值：好叫莉迪亚的这位太太朋友骂他的时候，能换个别的名目，别只说他是只放错了地方的虫豸。

===Original===
And Scylla no longer believed that her reserve of charity was an arsenal. She did not want Lydia if she could not tell her the story of the cup, draw on her learning, and on her instinct for tradition, which might have been created to meet the situation. Without that story her summer in the South was no story, and how often had Lydia been down with them in the wood? Philip once, had followed her there, uninvited, and found her singing them troubador songs. Had bawled jazz and almost dragged her away. Impulses cold, cruel, and insolent grew in Scylla, along with understanding perfecting itself.

===Chinese===
斯库拉也不再相信，自己那点善意的储备是一座武库了。若是不能把那只杯的故事讲给莉迪亚听，不能倚仗她的学问、她那份对传统的本能——那本能简直像专为应付这个局面造出来的——那么她要莉迪亚做什么呢。没有那个故事，她南方的夏天便不成其为故事；想当年，莉迪亚有多少回同他们一起下到林子里去？菲利普倒有一回，没人请，自己跟了去，撞见她正给他们唱游吟歌人的曲子。便扯开嗓子吼爵士乐，差点把她硬生生拽走。冰冷的、残忍的、傲慢的冲动在斯库拉心里滋长起来，与此同时，她的洞悉也在渐趋完满。

===Original===
A new aspect of the worst had arrived. They were already too accustomed to it. Had seen too many designs broken, whose assembly had been mysteries of harmony. Until they had forgotten unity, harvest ahead of vintage; forgotten that there could be any condition but emulation, advantage, and personal success. She despised herself because she had not the clean surgery to cut out memory and hope. As the story of the house could not be told without the wood, the house-party could not be described without the cup. As well talk politics to Picus as speak of the cup with Philip in the room.

===Chinese===
最坏的事端又翻出了新的一面。他们对这种事早已过分习惯。见过了太多被拆散的格局，那些格局当初拼合起来，本是和谐的秘仪。直到他们忘了浑然一体，忘了收获在酿酒之先；忘了世上除了一争高下、占尽便宜、个人功成之外，本来还可以有别的境况。她瞧不起自己，因为她下不了那干净利落的一刀，把记忆和希望剜除出去。正如这座宅子的故事离了林子讲不成，宅中聚客的情形离了那只杯也描画不成。与其当着菲利普谈那只杯，还不如对皮库斯谈政治。

===Original===
“What happened down South?” said Lydia. “London makes me ache for it. I hear the waves turning over﻿—don’t interrupt, Phil﻿—and the branches turning round in the wood.” Scylla thought: Concentrate on Carston. Make him funny﻿—with the fun left out. Nothing that she said held together, who had Picus and under Gault to tell to the proper person to hear it, *sœur douce amie*. Lydia must know that because of Philip she could not tell. Lydia had refused to dine alone with her. Scylla did not know the stupid scene he had made when Lydia had tried to go, until he had made love to her, and snatched a promise she did not dare break.

===Chinese===
“南方那边怎么样了？”莉迪亚说。“伦敦想得我心口发疼。我听得见浪头翻着身——菲尔，别打岔——听得见林子里的枝条打着旋儿。”斯库拉心想：专讲卡斯顿（Carston）。把他讲得滑稽——只是把乐趣抽掉的那种滑稽。她说什么都串不成篇，她本是有皮库斯、有高尔特土层（Gault）之下的那件东西要讲的，讲给那配听的人，*sœur douce amie*（温柔亲密的姊妹）。莉迪亚一定知道，只因菲利普在，她才讲不得。莉迪亚拒绝过单独同她吃饭。斯库拉却不知道，先前莉迪亚想走的时候，他闹过怎样一场蠢戏——直到向她求了欢，又攫去她一个不敢反悔的诺言方才罢休。

===Original===
Lydia knew and was not consoled. There might be news of Clarence. She was a jealous woman. Scylla had had Clarence to herself: had looked up at Philip, smiling. Already she knew what she had married, what they would become. Soon she would not be with Scylla’s people, or even in their world. And Scylla stayed in and walked out of it so airily. Soft, bitter, little laps of farseeing. The quickest thing to do was hate, before it was taken out of her in sorrow. Hadn’t Scylla come to triumph? Her husband’s delicious voice and vulgar accent enchanted and fretted her. His words and the beauty of his wrist as he lit Scylla’s cigarette. How could she keep him? And keep him Phil? Be sure of him and improve him? Possible or impossible, it was not her job. Who should have been advising Scylla, correcting and fortifying her.

===Chinese===
莉迪亚知道，却并没有因此得到安慰。说不定有克拉伦斯的消息。她是个善妒的女人。斯库拉曾经独占过克拉伦斯：她抬眼望着菲利普，微笑着。她早已知道自己嫁的是什么，知道他们俩会变成什么。要不了多久，她就不会再同斯库拉的那伙人在一起，甚至连他们的世界也进不得了。而斯库拉在这世界里住进去，又那般轻飘飘地走了出来。软的、苦的，一圈圈小小的先见之明。最快的办法是恨，趁恨还没有被悲哀从她身上扣除。斯库拉这不正是得胜还朝地来的吗？她丈夫那副悦耳的嗓音和俗气的腔调，既叫她着迷，又叫她煎熬。他的谈吐，还有他给斯库拉点烟时手腕那漂亮的一转。她怎么留得住他？怎么留得住他这个菲尔？怎么拿得稳他、又调教得好他？成也罢不成也罢，这都不是她的差事。她的差事本该是给斯库拉出主意，规劝她、扶助她。

===Original===
Exasperated, the lion’s paw fell, claws astretch.

===Chinese===
恼怒之下，狮子的一爪拍了下去，爪尖根根张开。

===Original===
What follows can be as well represented operatically﻿—it began:

===Chinese===
接下来的场面，不妨就用歌剧的路数来搬演——它是这样开场的：

===Original===
**Philip.** *Recitative.* “Lydia and I are often thinking of you, Scylla﻿—and I’m sure you won’t take us up wrong.”
**Lydia.** “We were both thinking if it is quite the thing for you to be there alone with all those men!”
**Scylla.** *Song.* “Felix is my chaperone, chaperone,” etc.
**Philip and Lydia.** *Duet.* “In the end it does not do, does not do,
 

 People know you for that kind of woman.”
**Scylla.** “What sort of a woman?”
**Philip and Lydia.** *Recit.* “We feel it since we married. It does not do, it does not do, to go against society.”
**Philip.** “I’ve seen a good deal of the world, you know﻿—perhaps not quite the same society as yours, but﻿—”
**Philip, Lydia and Scylla.** *Trio.* “People say﻿—”
“What do they say?”
“You know the things they say.”
“What have they said?”
“We’d rather not tell you and go into details.”
“Go into details!”
“You’re doing it for **my good**.”
**Philip and Lydia.** *Duet.* “Of course we are, of course we are.
 

 We wouldn’t hurt your feelings,
 

 **But﻿—**”
**Philip.** “I’m so fond of you, Scylla.”
**Lydia.** “We’re so fond of you, Scylla.
 

 
 **But﻿—**
 
 

 We’ve found it out, we’ve found it out.
 

 The world has reason on its side.”
**Scylla.** *Solo.* “What is the world?
 

 Lydia’s world was my world,
 

 And I don’t know Philip’s world.
 

 What reason has the world got, anyhow?”
**Philip and Lydia.** *Anthem.*
 **“It does not do.**
 
 

 **It does not do.**”
**Philip and Scylla.** *Duet.* “What good do these men do to you?”
 

 “What good do I do them?”
**Philip and Lydia.** *Quick recitative.* “But can’t you consider that everyone thinks that you sleep with each other in turn?”
**Philip, Lydia and Scylla.** *Trio.* “Including my brother?”
“Now, Scylla, be decent!”
“I am learning behaviour from you.”
“You’re so young,
So attractive﻿—”
“I am several years older than you.”
**Lydia.** “You were always a baby.”
**Philip.** “And always the lady.”

===Chinese===
**菲利普。** *宣叙调。*“莉迪亚和我常常惦记着你，斯库拉——相信你不会把我们的好意领会错了。”
**莉迪亚。**“我们俩都在想，你孤身一人同那么多男人待在那儿，到底像不像话！”
**斯库拉。** *咏叹调。*“菲利克斯给我当陪媪，当陪媪，”等等。
**菲利普与莉迪亚。** *二重唱。*“到头来总是不像话，不像话，
 

 人家认你就是那种女人。”
**斯库拉。**“哪种女人？”
**菲利普与莉迪亚。** *宣叙。*“我们打结婚起就有了这感觉。违逆社交体统，不像话，不像话。”
**菲利普。**“我也见过不少世面，你知道——兴许跟你们那一路的社会不完全是一回事，可是——”
**菲利普、莉迪亚与斯库拉。** *三重唱。*“人家说——”
“人家说什么？”
“你知道人家说的那些。”
“人家到底说了些什么？”
“我们还是不讲、也不细谈的好。”
“细谈呀！”
“你们这是为**我好**。”
**菲利普与莉迪亚。** *二重唱。*“我们当然是为你好，当然是为你好。
 

 我们不愿伤了你的感情，
 

 **可是——**”
**菲利普。**“我这么喜欢你，斯库拉。”
**莉迪亚。**“我们都这么喜欢你，斯库拉。
 

 
 **可是——**
 
 

 我们看出来了，我们看出来了。
 

 这世界站在道理那一边。”
**斯库拉。** *独唱。*“世界是什么？
 

 莉迪亚的世界原是我的世界，
 

 菲利普的世界我却不认得。
 

 说到底，这世界又占着个什么道理？”
**菲利普与莉迪亚。** *颂歌。*
 **“不像话。**
 
 

 不像话。**”
**菲利普与斯库拉。** *二重唱。*“这些男人于你有什么好处？”
 

 “那我于他们又有什么好处？”
**菲利普与莉迪亚。** *急板宣叙。*“可你就不能想一想，人人都当你们轮流同彼此睡觉？”
**菲利普、莉迪亚与斯库拉。** *三重唱。*“连我哥哥也算在内？”
“行了，斯库拉，放规矩点儿！”
“我正跟你们学规矩呢。”
“你这么年轻，
这么讨人喜欢——”
“我可比你大着好几岁呢。”
**莉迪亚。**“你从来就是个娃娃。”
**菲利普。**“也向来是位淑女。”

===Original===
Philip really said that, and when Scylla giggled, the string that tied them burned through and snapped. She remembered Picus at home: under Gault. A cup in a well: in a house. Out of India: in a book out of no man’s land.

===Chinese===
菲利普还真说了这话；斯库拉噗嗤一笑，维系着他们的那根弦便烧穿、绷断了。她想起在家乡的皮库斯：高尔特土层之下。井里的一只杯：在一座宅子里。来自印度：在一本出自无人之地的书里。

===Original===
> A shore like that, my dear,
 

 Lies where no man will steer,
 

 No maiden-land.

===Chinese===
> 那样的海岸，我亲爱的，
 

 躺在无人肯掌舵的地方，
 

 不是处女之邦。

===Original===
Most men steer there, and away before they have properly landed. “Land me where my friend and her fancy-man are waiting to bite.” She noticed how they hunted a single line as a double technique﻿—Lydia wanting to find out, Philip to defame. It infuriated her that she should be hurt.

===Chinese===
多数男人把船开到那儿，还没正经靠岸便又掉头而去。“把我放到我朋友和她的小情夫等着咬人的地方去吧。”她注意到，他们追猎同一句话头，用的却是双份的手法——莉迪亚要探出实情，菲利普要泼人脏水。让她火冒三丈的是，她居然会被刺伤。

===Original===
Lydia was saying:

===Chinese===
莉迪亚正说：

===Original===
“I am awfully fond of those boys, Scylla, but they’re *mal vus*.”

===Chinese===
“我是真心喜欢那几个男孩，斯库拉，可他们*mal vus*（名声不佳）。”

===Original===
“What is that?” (Don’t defend.)

===Chinese===
“那是什么意思？”（别辩解。）

===Original===
“Well, you know﻿—”

===Chinese===
“呃，你知道的——”

===Original===
“No, I don’t. Try again.”

===Chinese===
“不，我不知道。换个说法。”

===Original===
Lydia did:

===Chinese===
莉迪亚便换了个说法：

===Original===
“Why did you break up so soon? You said Felix had gone to Paris and you don’t seem to know about the others. Where’s Clarence?”

===Chinese===
“你们怎么散得那么早？你说菲利克斯去了巴黎，其余那几个的下落，你好像也不知道。克拉伦斯呢？”

===Original===
If she knew even that, she would have something to keep the old heartbreak company.

===Chinese===
哪怕她连这一点也知道，那桩旧伤心也就有个伴儿了。

===Original===
Philip was saying:

===Chinese===
菲利普正说：

===Original===
“Scylla, why don’t you marry Clarence: People say he’s a beauty, and it’s time you picked up a husband﻿—”

===Chinese===
“斯库拉，你怎么不嫁给克拉伦斯：人都说他生得漂亮，你也到了该捡一个丈夫的时候了——”

===Original===
“She wouldn’t,” said Lydia﻿—“despises Clarence. But she can’t go on like this.”

===Chinese===
“她才不肯呢，”莉迪亚说——“她看不起克拉伦斯。可她不能照这样过下去。”

===Original===
“Go on like **what**?” Philip answered her.

===Chinese===
“照**哪样**过下去？”菲利普回敬她。

===Original===
“You know what people say about a set with no real men in it.”

===Chinese===
“你知道人家怎么说一个圈子的——那里面没有一个真正的男人。”

===Original===
“What is a **real man**?”

===Chinese===
“什么才算**真正的男人**？”

===Original===
“They don’t amount to anything, and you know it. I’ve seen the world in my little way, and that sort don’t count. I think I’ve got Lydia out of that kind of thing. We mean to make a good business of things as we find them. Can’t finnick about with white hands, old standards, and fancy words these days. Don’t mean to, do we? And we shan’t get into quite the messes we might find if you asked us down South. Perhaps that’s why you don’t. And, honestly, I don’t know if I’d let Lydia go﻿—”

===Chinese===
“他们那号人成不了什么气候，这你是知道的。我也多少见过些世面，那一路人算不得数。我想我已经把莉迪亚从那一套里拽出来了。我们打算就着眼前的事，好好做成一笔生意。如今这年头，可不能再捧着一双白净的手，抱着老规矩、耍着漂亮字眼斤斤挑剔了。我们也无意那么干，对吧？再说，你若请我们下南方去，我们没准儿会撞上些烂摊子；不去，那样的浑水我们便蹚不着。没准儿你就是为这个才不请的。而且老实说，我都不知道我让不让莉迪亚去——”

===Original===
“If you mean that you’d find Ross having an affair with Nanna, you can go and look.”

===Chinese===
“你要是说会撞见罗斯（Ross）同娜娜（Nanna）有什么私情，那你尽管去看好了。”

===Original===
Philip went on:

===Chinese===
菲利普接着说：

===Original===
“You know we don’t mean that. If you’ll excuse me, but Lydia said the other day that you’re getting to think of nothing but sex﻿—”

===Chinese===
“你知道我们不是那个意思。恕我直言，莉迪亚前两天还说，你现在满脑子想的净是性事——”

===Original===
Insolent little cub. She had a last look at Lydia, twisting her wedding-ring.

===Chinese===
无礼的小崽子。她最后望了莉迪亚一眼——莉迪亚正捻着自己的结婚戒指。

===Original===
“Of course, I am,” she said. “I know something about it. Very naturally, now. I’ve been trying to tell you. We have all separated now because (not my brother, of course) we can’t decide which one of them shall marry me, and we’ve run away to think; I can’t make up my mind. Not Ross, or Picus. But I’ve decided not to look outside our set.”

===Chinese===
“我当然是，”她说。“这个嘛，我还略知一二。眼下再自然不过。我一直想告诉你们来着。我们现在全都散伙了，就因为（我哥哥当然不算）我们定不下他们当中哪一位该娶我，于是各自跑开去想一想；我拿不定主意。不是罗斯，也不是皮克（Pic）。不过我已经拿定主意，决不到我们这个圈子以外去找。”

===Original===
She saw the blood rising in Lydia’s face. Not a blush, a tide to the brain.

===Chinese===
她看见血涌上莉迪亚的脸。不是含羞的红晕，是直冲脑门的一片潮。

===Original===
Now I’ve done it. I’ve lied. I’ve hurt her. Considering my present relations with Clarence﻿—

===Chinese===
这下闯祸了。我说了谎。我伤了她。想一想我眼下同克拉伦斯的关系——

===Original===
Lydia was saying:

===Chinese===
莉迪亚正说：

===Original===
“I don’t know. There is something fatal about your life, Scylla.” She noticed that it excited Philip to think of her desired.

===Chinese===
“我说不上来。你的生活里带着一种致命的东西，斯库拉。”她注意到，一想到她被人渴求，菲利普便兴奋起来。

===Original===
A gulf had opened between them, on whose widening edge they shouted brutal farewells. They were telling her that her brother had given dishonoured cheques: that Picus had syphilis: Carston blackmailed: Ross was a satyr and a stunt painter. And Philip that Clarence had shown cowardice at the front. Then his wife turned on him a look of insanity, and Scylla saw a tiny thread of blood run out of her nostril. A posy stood in Felix’s wedding-present, a bowl of flint glass. Philip dashed the water of it on her forehead, and held the sweet scented names to her nose. Lydia struggled up and the bowl was knocked out of his hands and splintered. Scylla had to force herself to laugh, and not to say: “It’s a camp story I told you: invented it, spite for spite.” “I’m going,” she said.

===Chinese===
一道鸿沟在他们之间裂开，他们站在愈裂愈宽的沟沿上，吼着残忍的道别。他们告诉她：她哥哥开过空头支票：皮库斯得了梅毒：卡斯顿遭人敲诈：罗斯是个色情狂，外加一个耍噱头的画匠。菲利普还说克拉伦斯在前线上怯过阵。随后他妻子朝他转过一记癫狂的眼色，斯库拉看见一线细细的血从她鼻孔里淌出来。一小束花插在菲利克斯的结婚礼物——一只燧石玻璃碗——里。菲利普把碗里的水泼在她额头上，把那束甜香扑鼻的名字举到她鼻子底下。莉迪亚挣扎着起身，碗从他手里被打落，摔得粉碎。斯库拉只得逼着自己发笑，忍住不说：“那是我讲给你听的一个军营传闻：现编的，以毒攻毒。”“我走了。”她说。

===Original===
Lydia cried: “Is that all that happened?”

===Chinese===
莉迪亚喊道：“发生的事就只有这些吗？”

===Original===
Again she almost meant to say “No, it’s a long business. I came here to ask what you thought.” Then was damned if she would.

===Chinese===
她又几乎想说出“不，说来话长。我来，就是想问问你是怎么想的。”转念之间，她却死也不肯说了。

===Original===
She said: “What’s the good of my staying here? We shall all be back there soon. I’ll ask about the syphilis and the satyriasis. Does one put a notice in the papers about Felix’s cheques? Shall I tell Clarence to let you know how he escaped court-martial in spite of his seven wounds?”

===Chinese===
她说：“我留在这儿还有什么意思？我们不久都要回那边去的。梅毒和色情狂的事，我会去问个明白。菲利克斯的支票，要不要在报上登个启事？要不要我叫克拉伦斯告诉你，他带着七处伤，是怎么逃过军事法庭的？”

## V / 五、蜡像昼梦

===Original===
Picus was alone in his room, modelling the body of Scylla in wax. One of the little things he did. Clarence was a serious and accomplished painter, discovered and produced by Ross. Picus played about with wax, which grew more transparent as he touched it. Exceedingly powerful in body, he looked like wax, in a gauze-thin blue sweater rolled up his neck. He looked out of the window at the wood swimming in the midday heat, let out a little breath and waited an answer from the wood. He smiled and began to dance, like a marsh-bird, swinging up a leg, effortlessly, in any direction.

===Chinese===
皮库斯（Picus）独自待在房间里，用蜡捏塑斯库拉（Scylla）的身子。这是他常干的小玩意儿里的一件。克拉伦斯（Clarence）是位正经而有造诣的画家，由罗斯（Ross）发掘提携出来的。皮库斯则拿蜡耍弄，那蜡经他手一碰，越发透明。他体格极壮，穿一件卷到脖根的薄纱似的蓝毛衣，人倒像是蜡做的。他望着窗外泡在正午暑气里的树林，轻轻吐出一口气，等树林给他回音。他微微一笑，跳起舞来，像一只沼泽鸟，一条腿甩上去，朝哪个方向都全不费力。

===Original===
Then his face expressed pain. He put up his hands to his head and pressed them in. In a kind of despair, he turned and dug his nails in the wax of Scylla’s flesh.

===Chinese===
随即他的脸上现出痛楚。他抬起双手扣住头，往里摁。他带着一种绝望，转过身去，把指甲抠进斯库拉肉身的蜡里。

===Original===
Carston came upstairs to wash, bewildered by the dark stair, the corridor crossed with sun motes. He walked into Picus’s room by mistake. There he saw him, very gracious, in a room shabbier than his own, making the portrait of Scylla in wax. He saw brightness, nakedness, a toy. A liveliness of colour to remind him that she was a young woman alone among young men.

===Chinese===
卡斯顿（Carston）上楼来盥洗，被幽暗的楼梯、被阳光浮尘纵横交错的走廊弄得晕头转向。他走错了门，闯进皮库斯的房间。只见他风度翩然地待在一间比他自己那间更寒素的屋里，正用蜡塑斯库拉的像。他看见的是光亮、赤裸、一件玩物。一股鲜活的色彩，提醒他她是个独身混在一群青年男子中间的年轻女子。

===Original===
“I don’t like it,” said Picus, and broke it.﻿—“I’ll make another after tea.” Carston could have cried. The waste of richness, the shocking petulance, a toy that excited him shown and taken away. For a moment he had embraced Scylla. Another of the little things they did in their spare time.

===Chinese===
“我不喜欢它。”皮库斯说着，把它掰断了。——“喝过茶我再捏一个。”卡斯顿几乎要哭出来。那样丰美的东西就这样糟蹋了，那样惊人心意的乖张，一件撩得他心痒的玩物，亮出来又拿走了。有一瞬间，他分明把斯库拉拥在过怀里。这也是他们闲暇里常干的小玩意儿中的一件。

===Original===
Pushed out of his politeness, he said:

===Chinese===
被一股客气劲儿推出了口，他说：

===Original===
“You’re the one who discovered the cup, aren’t you?”

===Chinese===
“发现那只杯的人是你，对吧？”

===Original===
“No,” said Picus, “I only thought of the spear to poke about with. It was Felix’s find.”

===Chinese===
“不是，”皮库斯说，“拿矛去捅着探的念头倒是我想出来的。是菲利克斯（Felix）找到的。”

===Original===
“Miss Taverner’s brother seems a bit upset about it.”

===Chinese===
“塔弗纳（Taverner）小姐的弟弟好像为这事有点不痛快。”

===Original===
“Does he?”

===Chinese===
“是吗？”

===Original===
“You shouldn’t have broken that statue.”

===Chinese===
“你不该把那座像掰了。”

===Original===
Picus covered up both statements like a perfect young gentleman, rather a stupid one. It occurred to Carston that he was stupid; also that perhaps it had scandalised him to have shown Scylla naked to a stranger, and hoped it was that.

===Chinese===
皮库斯把那两句话都敷衍了过去，像一位十全十美的年轻绅士——毋宁说，相当蠢的一位。卡斯顿忽然觉得他蠢；又想，把斯库拉的裸体亮给一个外人看，他说不定真觉得有伤体统，但愿是这么回事。

===Original===
They went down to lunch. It was his first lunch, but he felt as though never in his life had he done anything but eat there. Once he had lived in America, once he had come to Europe, but that did not count any more. That theatre was as another earth, and the plays were not the prologue to his play. For this play there had been no rehearsal and he did not know his part. Or, if he had a part, he had to improvise it, and it must be a good part. Lost in a green transparent world, he was blind. Beginning to see in a new way he disliked, a seeing like jealousy, without arrangement; principally a sensibility about Scylla, likely to become a fury of desire. He remembered its modest beginnings the night before, his rejection of it on further acquaintance with her brother. That it had started again in Picus. Somebody said: “What do we do this afternoon?” The heat answered that. Laid down on the verandah in a wicker chair like a shell, he lay still, face to face with the wood. One by one the others disappeared.

===Chinese===
他们下楼去吃午饭。这是他来后的头一顿午饭，可他只觉自己这辈子仿佛没干过别的，净在这儿吃饭了。他曾在美国住过，也曾来过欧洲，可那些都不作数了。那座剧场恍如另一颗地球，那些戏也不是他这出戏的序幕。这出戏没有排演过，他不知道自己的角色。或者说，纵然有个角色，他也得临场自编，而且非编出个好角色不可。迷失在一个绿色透明世界里，他是瞎的。他开始用一种自己厌恶的新方式看东西，一种近于嫉妒的看，毫无章法；核心是对斯库拉的一种敏感，眼看要化作欲望的狂怒。他记得前一夜它初起时还何等收敛，待同她弟弟进一步相熟，他还把它摒退过。——如今它又在皮库斯身上发作起来。有人说：“今天下午咱们干什么？”暑热替他们作了答。他摊在游廊上一把贝壳似的柳条椅里，一动不动，与树林面面相对。其余的人一个接一个走散了。

---

===Original===
Ross went up the hill, carrying his painting things. The place he wanted could not be seen from any shade there was on the down-top. He planted his easel in the full light.

===Chinese===
罗斯背着画具上了山。他要画的那地方，从丘陵顶上任何一处荫凉里都望不见。他把画架支在烈日直晒之下。

===Original===
The cliffs down the coast were too good-looking. He chose the somberest patch of barn and field in the next valley and drew it hard, his shirtsleeves rolled up his amber arms, his back square to the house in the wood, several hundred feet together.

===Chinese===
沿岸那几道悬崖太俊俏了。他挑了下一道山谷里最晦暗的一角谷仓和田地，落笔沉沉地画，衬衫袖子卷上琥珀色的胳膊，背脊端端正正对着树林里那座宅子——连同隔开它们的几百英尺坡地。

===Original===
Presently he noticed that it was becoming difficult always to distinguish between a sheep and a shrub, and that that meant thunder. With his back to the house and the wood he was being stopped working from the other side. He drew in a tree-shape rather hard. The white haze gathered. The more he looked, the less he saw. Instead, he began to see shapes in his head, not outside it, an exercise he avoided, because it interfered with precision of hand. Unwillingly he felt that he would have to return before he meant to, to a place where there was a martyred ass called Clarence, lying alone for a moment in a verandah, a little distance from a young American, who was keeping remarkably silent. Whom his instincts were against. Not because he disliked him, but because the town-bred contact between them had died. They were all stuck down there in a bewilderment, which had happened because they had forgotten the duties of hospitality and had left it to Scylla to fetch the stranger from Starn. If they had not done that, two of the party would have died of the hedgehogs, or else come straight over to them without raking up a well. Not that he was sorry that it had happened. Then he whistled as he drew, out of tune, but as though he was loving something. No nonsense about being the thing he loved, but like a lover, aware of the presence of what he loved everywhere.

===Chinese===
不久他注意到，要回回分清一只羊和一丛灌木越来越难，而那意味着雷雨要来。他背对着宅子和树林，活计却从另一头被人搅了。他把一株树的形状描得略重了些。白茫茫的霾聚拢起来。他越看，看见的越少。反倒开始看见自己脑子里的形，不是外头的形——这门功课他向来躲着，因为它碍手上的精准。他不情愿地觉出，自己怕是得比原定的早些回去，回到有一头受难的驴子、名叫克拉伦斯的地方去；那驴此刻正独自在游廊上躺一小会儿，离一个从头到尾异常沉默的美国青年不远。而他的本能对那青年是抵制的。不是因为他讨厌他，而是因为他们之间那点城市人气息相投的来往已经死了。他们全都陷在那底下的一场惶惑里，起因是他们忘了待客的本分，把去斯塔恩（Starn）接陌生人的事丢给了斯库拉一个人。若不是那样，这一行人里早有两个要死在刺猬上头，再不然，也会径直寻到他们跟前，犯不着扒出一口井来。他倒并不惋惜出了这桩事。于是他一边画一边吹起口哨，走了调，却像是在爱着什么。不是那种非要变成所爱之物的胡闹，而是像个情人，处处觉出所爱之物的临在。

===Original===
There was a hard, explosive sound. Several mixed noises. A bird tore out of a thicket and crossed an open space, indirectly, frantically, and disappeared. He imitated its call and burst out laughing. “Woodpecker up to his tricks again.” Then he went back to his work, straining his eyes.

===Chinese===
一声爆响，又硬又炸。接着几样声响混作一团。一只鸟从树丛里猛蹿出来，曲曲折折、慌慌张张地掠过一片空地，不见了。他学了一声鸟叫，随即放声大笑。“啄木鸟又耍他的把戏了。”然后他回到活计上，眯起眼睛使劲看。

---

===Original===
In the verandah, Clarence slept. He dreamed that he was walking, at night, on a thin spit of rock across the sea, Picus’s slender height and great weight against his shoulders, in his arms. Picus was dead, and he was glad he was dead and it was over. The difficulty was to get rid of the body, which was coming alive somewhere else and following him. It could only be got rid of at the end of the rock, and he could not go on much longer like that. There were dark hills round the sea, and in them was the living Picus, not his at all, but another, the real one. It was such a bother, his feet were covered with blood. It wasn’t till the dead Picus was in the sea, that the real one would come out of the hills and play with him. No use waiting for day, because it was always dark in that country.

===Chinese===
游廊上，克拉伦斯睡着了。他梦见自己夜里走在一道细细的岩岬上，岩岬横跨海面，皮库斯那细挑而沉重的身子压在他肩头、抱在他怀里。皮库斯死了，他庆幸他死了，事情总算完了。难办的是怎么处置这具尸身——它正在别的什么地方活转过来，缀在他后面。只有到岩岬尽头才能把它甩脱，而像这样他撑不了多久了。海的四周环着黑黢黢的群山，山里有一个活着的皮库斯，压根不是他的那个，是另一个，真的那一个。真叫人心烦，他双脚沾满了血。要等死了的那个皮库斯落进海里，真的那个才肯从山里出来陪他玩。等天亮是没用的，因为在那片国土上永远是黑夜。

===Original===
He often went there when he was asleep, often with a dead bird, Picus the Woodpecker, in his hand and in his arms. Sometimes it was an image for Picus. Sometimes him. There came a point when he would say: “This is a play, made out of my wishes and my disappointment. Truth is quite different. I am unhappy because the boy has things wrong with his character, because he has things wrong with his inside. Or, because we all think, somehow, that Picus is a bad lot.” After this correction there would come the final idea that saw behind these images and their rationalisation another truth. He stirred, shifted and fell asleep again, not knowing at all that Carston, awake, was wondering why he seemed so wretched, and why he had dictated them and taken offence at lunch. That Carston liked him, and admired his good looks, who could only see how worn they were beside the American’s ageless set trimness.

===Chinese===
他一睡着就常去那儿，手里怀里常捧着一只死鸟——啄木鸟皮库斯。有时那不过是皮库斯的一个影像。有时就是他本人。每到某一处，他总会说：“这是一出戏，拿我的愿望和我的失望编成的。真相全然是另一回事。我不快活，是因为这孩子的性子有毛病，是因为他的内里有毛病。再不然，是因为我们大家不知怎么都认定，皮库斯不是个好东西。”这样纠正过之后，又会来了最后一个念头，它看穿这些影像连同它们的种种自圆其说，看出后头另有一个真相。他动了动，翻个身，又睡着了，全然不知醒着的卡斯顿正在纳闷：他为什么显得这般愁苦，为什么午饭时他发号施令、动辄见怪。也全然不知卡斯顿是喜欢他的，还赞赏他那副好相貌——尽管那相貌在卡斯顿眼里，比着美国人那副不见年纪、绷硬齐整的利落，只显得多么磨损。

===Original===
He dreamt again: another Picus came walking up the rock-spit, carrying a glass dish which was the cup of the Sanc-Grail, saying: “It’s the *lapis exilii*, the stone of exile. What I’m walking on is the *lapis exilis*, the slender stone. All the same, my dear.” Then the neat reminder that no grail texts were clear what the thing was really called. Then his private fancy to call it the *lapis exultationis*, the stone of joy. That the thing had never existed. The joy-stone. Freud again. Had he ordered more wax for Picus to play with? A letter with a stamp like a black star, shifting along a river which carried the London post. Too slowly. It must get there quick, or Picus would be angry and say he hadn’t sent it. He woke to remember that that had been an old quarrel, and the stuff had come. Also that he ought to talk to Carston, wide awake in a basket chair, five stone pillars away. That he was feeling horribly shy, raw, ill-adjusted, sick to assure himself that the others thought he was good for something. Had the American seen through him? After all, he’d seen war. Half an hour’s more sleep. Perhaps the dreams would be more comfortable, or wouldn’t come.

===Chinese===
他又做梦了：另一个皮库斯走上岩岬来，捧着一只玻璃盘，正是圣所圣杯（Sanc-Grail）的那只杯，说道：“这是*lapis exilii*，流放之石。我脚下走的这条是*lapis exilis*，纤细之石。其实是一回事，亲爱的。”随后是那句利落的提醒：没有一部圣杯文献说得清这东西究竟叫什么。随后又是他私自的遐想，想把它唤作*lapis exultationis*，欢悦之石。说这东西从未存在过。欢悦之石。又是弗洛伊德（Freud）。他订了蜡没有，再订些给皮库斯玩？一封信，贴着一颗黑星似的邮票，顺着一条载运伦敦邮班的河慢慢挪。太慢了。它得快些到，不然皮库斯要恼，要说他压根儿没寄。他醒了，想起那原是一桩旧日的口角，蜡也早到过了。还想起他该去同卡斯顿谈谈——那人正醒着，坐在五根石柱开外的柳条篮椅里。想起自己此刻羞怯得厉害，皮肉生疼，格格不入，急煎煎地要确认旁人还当他有几分用处。那美国人把他看穿了吗？毕竟，他是见过战争的。再睡半个钟头。也许这回的梦会好受些，再不然，干脆别来。

===Original===
Carston was wondering if he was expected to come over and talk. He liked to hear Clarence talk about war. He had seen some rough stuff himself in Russia. A good soldier the man must have been. Wondered why he hadn’t stuck to it, and was now rather overdoing the art business. The others did not overdo it. Quite a good painter, too. Then he saw Scylla in the treetops. A limb of ilex, detached from the main height and formed perfectly. Lifted up, glittering in the insolent sky. She was upstairs, broken in pieces, in preposterously pretty, sexual wax. Picus might be there, making another. He’d go up and see. Creep in, if he wasn’t allowed to enjoy it.

===Chinese===
卡斯顿则在琢磨，人家是指望他过去搭话呢，还是不指望。他爱听克拉伦斯谈战争。他自己在俄国也见过些狠辣场面。这人当年想必是一员好兵。他不明白他为什么不接着当下去，如今倒把艺术这行当弄得有些过火。旁人可没有过火。他也算得上一位不错的画家。随后他在树梢上看见了斯库拉。一枝圣栎的枝柯，脱开了主干的巍峨，独自长得完满无缺。被高高擎起，在骄横的天光里熠熠生辉。而她在楼上，碎成了几块，躺在漂亮得不像话、满带肉欲的蜡里。皮库斯也许正在那儿，再捏一个。他要上去看看。要是人家不许他赏玩，就溜进去。

===Original===
Almost as good as having the girl, to have that thing of her in wax. This was as far as he got. It was quite true that the statue would have done as well. Desire in Carston was almost mental, a redecoration of his memories. Only at the moment he was between the two, the statuette and the girl, the shoulders he saw in leaf and wax and flesh, and was troubled by the repeats.

===Chinese===
把她那样一件蜡像弄到手，差不多跟把姑娘本人弄到手一样好。他的念头只走到这一步。说实在的，那座蜡像原本也尽够用了。卡斯顿的欲望几乎是精神性的，是给他记忆来的一场重新装修。只是此刻他悬在两者之间——蜡像和姑娘之间，那对肩膀他一会儿见它生在枝叶里，一会儿在蜡里，一会儿在肉里，来回的重影搅得他不安。

===Original===
While Clarence, asleep again, dreamed he was meeting Picus as he had met him in the war, wearing his shrapnel helmet, a queer glass dish someone had found in a well. Rather a worry.

===Chinese===
而克拉伦斯这边又睡着了，梦见自己同皮库斯重逢，一如战争年月里的初遇：皮库斯戴着他的弹片盔——一只不知谁从井里寻出来的古怪玻璃盘。真有点叫人操心。

===Original===
“Big magic,” said Picus. He was a boy then, his smile already gracious and timid, contrasting with a loose, haughty walk. He had said, laughing: “If you take it off, off comes my hair.” That was important. The queer fairy cup his bird wore. Some day Picus would take off his cap to him. He woke up. Something had broken in him, the sense of wrong adjustment was easier. It would come back, but now it was perfectly easy to talk to Carston, by this time also anxious to bridge the gulf between lunch and tea.

===Chinese===
“大魔法。”皮库斯说。那时他还是个少年，笑起来已经这样温文而怯生，同他那散漫高傲的步态恰成对照。他当时笑着说：“你要是把它摘下来，我的头发也跟着下来。”这一节要紧。他的鸟儿头上戴的那只古怪仙杯。有朝一日，皮库斯会向他脱帽。他醒了。他身子里有什么东西破了，那种错位感松快了些。它还会回来，可眼下同卡斯顿说话已经全不费难——这位到这时也正急着，要在午饭和茶点之间的鸿沟上搭一座桥。

## VI / 六、记忆之钥

===Original===
Tea was a reasonable meal, with a real human being at it, the doctor having come over from Starn to attend to Picus’s health. Carston held his attention, improvising brilliantly on aspects of his native land, wondering if he could interpret Scylla’s cordiality into the beginnings of desire. Quick work, he knew, but life in the infernal stillness was going at a pace that had New York beat. It became the doctor’s turn to talk. Carston noticed how they played in turns, the second guest after the first. “Pass the buns,” said Felix. That was the cue. Carston listened to stories of medical practice in a remote district; after a time to an accompaniment he did not at first locate. Later that it was Picus ringing with a spoon on his medicine glass.

===Chinese===
茶点是一顿像样儿的饭食，席上还坐着一位货真价实的人类——医生从斯塔恩（Starn）过来照看皮库斯（Picus）的身体。卡斯顿（Carston）占住了他的注意力，就自己故土的种种光景即兴发挥，说得花团锦簇，一边琢磨着，能不能把斯库拉（Scylla）的热络解读成欲望的开端。他晓得这是快功，可这地狱般寂静里的日子，正跑着一种连纽约都得认输的速度。轮到医生开口了。卡斯顿注意到他们怎样轮流登场，第一位客人之后是第二位。“小圆面包递一递。”菲利克斯（Felix）说。这就是引子。卡斯顿听着偏远乡间行医的故事；过了一阵，听出底下配着一层伴奏，起初没找着出处，后来才明白，是皮库斯拿一把匙子敲他的药杯。

===Original===
The doctor said:

===Chinese===
医生说：

===Original===
“I don’t wonder you two left Tollerdown. It’s a cheerless place at best. I only knew it in winter, going out there to deliver the shepherd’s wife. So I think of it as the darkest place that exists.”

===Chinese===
“难怪你们俩搬出了托勒当（Tollerdown）。那地方往好里说也是一片凄清。我只在冬天见识过它——出诊去给牧羊人的老婆接生。所以在我心里，它就是天底下最黑的地方。”

===Original===
Scylla answered: “I know. Even now when it is burnt white. I think of it the only time I was there in winter, in a storm. Wind roaring over the flint-crop and snow whirling. Lying an instant and vanishing.”

===Chinese===
斯库拉答道：“我知道。哪怕如今它烧得一片白。我记住它的，是唯一一次冬天待在那儿，赶上一场风暴。风在燧石茬上吼，雪打着旋。落下一瞬，又没了。”

===Original===
Ross said: “**Be prepared for lambing**﻿—You hear them mewing in the dark, and see a light in a wooden box on wheels, and out comes a shepherd, with his hands covered with blood.”

===Chinese===
罗斯（Ross）说：“**接羔的时节什么都得备齐**——你听见它们在黑暗里咩咩叫，看见一辆带轮子的木箱里透出一盏灯，钻出来一个牧羊人，两只手糊满了血。”

===Original===
The doctor said:

===Chinese===
医生说：

===Original===
“Show me the cup you got out of the well.” And when he had looked at it: “The luck of the country’s with you. I’m glad to find a few Roman pots. It isn’t glass at all, too heavy. I think it’s jade. It may have been set once. I tell you, it might have been the cup of a chalice.” Intelligent interest. Carston felt quite friendly now towards the thing. The others were giving polite attention. Five people at once thinking about a spear. No, six. He was.

===Chinese===
“把你们从井里捞的那只杯拿来我瞧瞧。”等他看过之后：“这一乡的运气全站在你们这边。能寻出几件罗马罐子，我很高兴。这压根儿不是玻璃，太沉了。我看是玉。兴许原先还镶过托座。我告诉你们，它说不定当过一只圣餐杯上的那口杯。”一番有见识的兴味。卡斯顿此刻对那东西已颇有好感。其余的人也都礼数周到地听着。五个人同时想着一杆矛。不，六个。他也在想。

===Original===
“One has time to remember things, shooting about this country in a Ford. Do you know it makes me think of what I remember of the cup of the Sanc-Grail?”

===Chinese===
“开着福特（Ford）车在这一带荒地里窜，人有大把工夫回想旧事。你们可知道，这叫我想起了我记得的圣所圣杯（Sanc-Grail）的那只杯？”

===Original===
Picus said, meekly: “What was that?”

===Chinese===
皮库斯乖顺地说：“那是怎么一回事？”

===Original===
Carston thought: How was that camp, or wasn’t it? Would one of them pick up the challenge? Of course, it was a challenge. Ross said: “That’s a long story,” but Scylla leaned forward, excited, and said: “The best way to get that story out is for everyone to say what he thinks or feels or remembers. The Freud game really. Start, Felix!”

===Chinese===
卡斯顿心想：这话里有没有机锋？他们中间会不会有谁接下这挑战？这当然是挑战。罗斯说：“那说来话长。”斯库拉却身子前倾，兴奋起来，说：“要把那故事掏出来，最好的法子就是人人都说说自己怎么想、怎么觉、记得什么。其实就是弗洛伊德（Freud）游戏。开个头吧，菲利克斯！”

===Original===
“Tennyson,” said Felix.

===Chinese===
“丁尼生（Tennyson）。”菲利克斯说。

===Original===
“Oh, my dear,” said Clarence, “those awful pre-Raphaelite pictures put me off it long ago.”

===Chinese===
“哦，亲爱的，”克拉伦斯（Clarence）说，“那些吓人的拉斐尔前派（pre-Raphaelite）绘画，早就把我的胃口败光了。”

===Original===
Ross said: “A mass said at Corbenic.”

===Chinese===
罗斯说：“科尔本尼克（Corbenic）的一场弥撒。”

===Original===
“Wagner,” said the doctor.

===Chinese===
“瓦格纳（Wagner）。”医生说。

===Original===
“A girl carrying it,” said Carston, staring at Scylla and trying to play.

===Chinese===
“一个捧着它的姑娘。”卡斯顿说，眼睛盯着斯库拉，努力入戏。

===Original===
Scylla said: “*Quod inferius, sicut superius est.*”

===Chinese===
斯库拉说：“*Quod inferius, sicut superius est.*（在下者，一如在上者。）”

===Original===
Picus said: “You haven’t told me much.”

===Chinese===
皮库斯说：“你们没告诉我多少东西。”

===Original===
“Second round,” said Scylla﻿—“people enlarge on what they said before.”

===Chinese===
“第二轮，”斯库拉说——“各位把方才说的铺开来讲。”

===Original===
“I said Tennyson,” said Felix, “because I hate the Celtic Twilight. And nearly all its works. I hate it because it’s a false way of telling about something that exists. No, a messy way. Responsible for the world’s worst art. Now and then it nearly comes off. Milton left it alone, and I don’t blame him. Tennyson made it idiotic with his temperance knights. Fixed it, too, enough for parody. Killed the unstated thing which I don’t mind telling you scares me.”

===Chinese===
“我说丁尼生，”菲利克斯说，“因为我恨凯尔特黄昏（Celtic Twilight）。连同它差不多全部的产品。我恨它，是因为它是对一件实有之物的不实讲法。不，是讲得邋遢。天底下最坏的艺术全是它造的孽。偶尔它也几乎要成。弥尔顿（Milton）不沾它，我不怪他。丁尼生拿他那些戒酒骑士把它弄成了傻子，还把它钉死了，钉得尽够人家拿去打趣。他把那件说不得的东西杀了——不怕告诉你们，那东西是叫我害怕的。”

===Original===
Clarence said: “I agree with Felix. I can’t stand bad drawing.”

===Chinese===
克拉伦斯说：“我同意菲利克斯。糟透的画工我看不得。”

===Original===
Ross said: “At Corbenic, wherever that was, there was a different mass. It may have been the real thing.”

===Chinese===
罗斯说：“在科尔本尼克——不管那是哪儿——做过一场不一样的弥撒。说不定那才是真的。”

===Original===
The doctor said: “Parsival is like a great religious service to me.”

===Chinese===
医生说：“《帕西法尔》（*Parsival*）之于我，就像一场盛大的宗教仪式。”

===Original===
Carston, embarrassed at his turn coming, saw their pained faces. He said: “I supposed the girl who carried it was the female spirit of life.”

===Chinese===
轮到自己时，卡斯顿窘了，看见他们一张张发苦的脸。他说：“我猜那捧杯的姑娘，是生命的女性精魂。”

===Original===
Scylla said: “I quote again: ‘*Here lies the Woodpecker who was Zeus.*’ ”

===Chinese===
斯库拉说：“我再引一句：‘*Here lies the Woodpecker who was Zeus.*（这里长眠着曾是宙斯的啄木鸟。）’”

===Original===
“Thank you,” said Picus.

===Chinese===
“多谢。”皮库斯说。

===Original===
Later, Carston asked her to take him for a walk. The doctor went with them to the gate, and she asked him what he thought about Picus’s health.

===Chinese===
稍后，卡斯顿请她带自己出去走走。医生陪他们走到大门口，她问他对皮库斯的身子怎么看。

===Original===
“Everything is wrong, and nothing,” he said.﻿—“I don’t mean by that that he invents it. His aches and pains are a mask that conceals something. What that is, I’ve never been able to find out﻿—”

===Chinese===
“什么都坏了，又什么都没坏。”他说。——“我这话不是说他瞎编。他那些疼痛是一副面具，遮着一样东西。是什么，我从来没能查出来——”

===Original===
“Does Clarence know?”

===Chinese===
“克拉伦斯知道吗？”

===Original===
“I shouldn’t care myself to know too much about Picus. Despair’s a bad bedfellow.”

===Chinese===
“换了我，是不想知道皮库斯太多的。绝望是个坏床伴。”

===Original===
Scylla said: “We know what despair is.” As if she were saying that she knew how to take a temperature. Carston went with her down the wood to the sea. Twenty-four hours before he had been alone with her for the first time. Alone with her the second time, he was almost in pain because he wished to use the moment, and did not know how. The more he planned the less he’d be able to do, who had rarely failed with women. Now the sun struck aslant, the light-chequers broadened into patches. It was damp and delicious. The evening birds were tuning up. A little sympathy is generally judicious.

===Chinese===
斯库拉说：“我们知道绝望是什么。”那口气，就像在说她知道怎么给人量体温。卡斯顿随她穿过树林下到海边。二十四小时前，他头一回同她单独相处。这第二回单独在一起，他几乎觉得痛苦，因为他想利用这个时刻，却不知从何利用。他越筹划，便越将一事无成——他这个在女人身上难得失手的人。此刻阳光斜射下来，光的棋格放宽成一块块斑。空气潮润而甘美。晚起的鸟儿正在调嗓子。一点点同情，通常总是明智的。

===Original===
“I took a walk this morning with your brother. He seemed troubled by what you found yesterday. Even now, after the talk at tea, I’m not clear what it is all about.”

===Chinese===
“今天上午我同你弟弟散了一回步。他好像为你们昨天找到的东西心神不宁。就是现在，谈过茶点上的那一席话，我还是不明白这一切究竟是怎么回事。”

===Original===
Not a hint of his sex had crossed her mind. An American boy, very polished and friendly. No reason not to tell him anything there was to tell.

===Chinese===
他的男性身份没有在她心里掠过一丝影子。一个美国青年，十分光鲜和气。凡是有可讲的，没有道理不讲给他听。

===Original===
For the third time Carston heard the sentence: “That’s a long story. You must help me to explain.”

===Chinese===
卡斯顿第三次听见这句话：“那说来话长。你得帮我解释。”

===Original===
He answered: “You said two things at tea. The Latin bit, which means, I think, that the things underneath are the same as the things on top. And something I don’t get at all: ‘*Here lies the Woodpecker who was Zeus.*’ ”

===Chinese===
他答道：“茶点上你说了两样东西。一段拉丁文，我想它的意思是，底下的东西和上面的东西是一回事。还有一样我全然不懂：‘*这里长眠着曾是宙斯的啄木鸟。*’”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“是啊。”

===Original===
“Then you said another thing﻿—you said that you all knew what despair is. How can that be true?”

===Chinese===
“后来你还说了另一件——你们说，你们全都知道绝望是什么。这怎么会是真的？”

===Original===
Scylla said: “Well, I take it that we have to know everything about being lost.”

===Chinese===
斯库拉说：“这么说吧，我认为我们不得不把‘迷失’这件事的里里外外都弄个明白。”

===Original===
Lost. He did not get that. If ever there existed a group sure of themselves. He mentioned it.

===Chinese===
迷失。这一点他没有懂。天底下要真有一伙把自家拿得稳稳的人，便是他们了。他把这话说了出来。

===Original===
“Swank,” she said, “and instinct. To cover quite intolerable pain. You see we know between us pretty well all there is to know. That’s why we rag all the time. To keep things clean, and because it’s the only gentlemanly thing to do. We have our jokes, our senses, and our moments of illumination which always take a turn for the worse. See? We live fast and are always having adventures, adventures which are like patterns of another adventure going on somewhere else all the time. A very different sort of affair, a state suggested if you like in a good work of art. The things down here seem hints of it, but there is nothing to make us sure that it is a reality. Let alone that it is worth what it costs us. Quite the contrary. We get into trouble over it, it runs after us, runs away from us, runs away with us, makes fun of us and fools of us. Because of it we have no money, and the wrong lovers, and our instinct for power is starved. For we come of families which have never been without power before. And the name for all this is our subconscious minds. And between Freud and Aquinas, I’ve managed to tell you about it completely wrong. For another of its names is intellectual beauty, and another, the peace of God.”

===Chinese===
“那是摆派头，”她说，“再加上本能。为了盖住一种实在受不了的疼。你瞧，我们几个凑起来，差不多天底下的学问都齐了。所以我们才整天互相打趣。为的是把事情保持干净，也因为这是唯一称得上绅士的做法。我们有我们的玩笑，我们的感官，还有我们那些灵光乍现的时刻——那些时刻总是一转眼就转坏。明白吗？我们日子过得飞快，奇遇不断；那些奇遇像另一场奇遇的底样，那一场时时刻刻在别的什么地方上演着。全然是另一种事务，一种——你愿意这么说的话——在一部好艺术品里暗示出来的境界。这底下的一切事物像是它的提示，可没有什么能叫我们确信它是实在的。更别提它值不值我们付出的代价了。恰恰相反。我们为它惹祸，它追我们，躲我们，把我们拐跑，拿我们打趣，把我们当傻瓜耍。因为它，我们没有钱，情人也不对路，我们对权力的本能活活饿着。因为我们出身的人家，此前从来不曾缺过权力。而这一切的名目，就是我们的潜意识。夹在弗洛伊德和阿奎那（Aquinas）之间，我居然把这事给你讲了个一塌糊涂。因为它另有一个名字，叫智性之美，还有一个，叫上帝的安宁。”

===Original===
“D’you believe in God?”

===Chinese===
“你们信上帝吗？”

===Original===
“I don’t know. All we do know is what happens to faith based on catch-as-catch-can visions.”

===Chinese===
“不知道。我们唯一知道的，是把信仰押在随手擒来的异象上，后来会落得什么。”

===Original===
“Weren’t all religions based on that?”

===Chinese===
“一切宗教不都是从那上头起的家吗？”

===Original===
“They were, and look at them! But now you see why we felt we were being laughed at, dangerously, when we lifted that cup out of a well on the point of a spear?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，瞧瞧它们如今这样子！这下你该明白了，当初我们把那只杯挑在一杆矛尖上、从井里捞上来的时候，为什么觉得我们正被人笑话着——而且笑得危险？”

===Original===
Carston pulled himself together. “What did you mean by the other thing: ‘*Here lies the Woodpecker who was Zeus*’?”

===Chinese===
卡斯顿定了定神。“另外那句又是怎么回事：‘*这里长眠着曾是宙斯的啄木鸟*’？”

===Original===
“A little poetry, a little witchery, a little joke. It’s the same thing as I said before. Now I’ll tell you something worse than what I said before.

===Chinese===
“一点诗，一点妖术，一点玩笑。同我方才说的是一回事。现在我要告诉你一件比刚才说的更厉害的。

===Original===
“Along with faith fit for people like us, and good taste which are where morals end, there is no goodwill left anywhere in the world. Which started to go first, or if they all went together, or which pushed the other out, I don’t know. I’ve an idea that something else, a principal we haven’t named yet, got rid of the lot.”

===Chinese===
“同配得上我们这号人的信仰一起，同好趣味——道德到了头便是好趣味——一起，这世上的善意已经荡然无存。是哪一样先走的，还是它们一齐走的，还是谁把谁挤出去的，我不知道。我有个想法：另有一样东西，一条我们还没给它起过名字的原则，把这一窝全收拾掉了。”

===Original===
Beginnings for an erotic conversation.

===Chinese===
这算是情欲谈话的一个开头。

===Original===
A turn of exasperation seized him. She was leaning on the red arm of a pine tree which stood by itself outside the wood, a crooked blue mushroom moulded by the wind. The scent from its cap mixed with the smell of wind off the tide-stripped rocks.

===Chinese===
一阵焦躁攫住了他。她正倚着一棵孤零零立在树林外的松树的赤红臂膀——那树让风捏成了一柄歪脖子的蓝蘑菇。伞盖上的气味，混着风从潮水剥光的礁石上带来的咸腥。

===Original===
